        // ⚠ THE CLIP TAKES THE SAME OFFSET AS THE CONTENT.  It is derived in WORLD
        // space by `compose`, while what it cuts has already been moved by the
        // camera and the viewport — so applying it raw cut the wrong region, and
        // a pan of more than the node's own width cut ALL of it: a clipped panel
        // silently disappeared the moment the camera moved.  A6 and P2 each
        // shipped green because no test moved a camera across a clip.
        if n.nd_clipped {
            k0 = (n.nd_cx0 + off.p_x) as integer;
            k1 = (n.nd_cx1 + off.p_x) as integer;
            l0 = (n.nd_cy0 + off.p_y) as integer;
            l1 = (n.nd_cy1 + off.p_y) as integer;
            if k0 > x0 { x0 = k0; }
            if l0 > y0 { y0 = l0; }
            if k1 < x1 { x1 = k1; }
            if l1 < y1 { y1 = l1; }
        }
        // The view's own rect, narrowed the same way.  An unbounded view (width
        // or height 0) confines nothing, which is the unsplit stage.
        if vpw > 0 and vph > 0 {
            if vpx > x0 { x0 = vpx; }
            if vpy > y0 { y0 = vpy; }
            if vpx + vpw < x1 { x1 = vpx + vpw; }
            if vpy + vph < y1 { y1 = vpy + vph; }
        }
        for py in y0..y1 {
            for px in x0..x1 {
                d = cv.get_pixel(px, py);
                dr = (d >> 16) & 255;
                dg = (d >> 8) & 255;
                db = d & 255;
                cv.set_pixel(px, py, 0xff000000
                    | (over(sr, dr, ia) << 16)
                    | (over(sg, dg, ia) << 8)
                    | over(sb, db, ia));
            }
        }
    }
}


// ── P2: the camera ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// ⚠ THE CAMERA IS APPLIED AT DRAW TIME, NEVER BAKED INTO NODE POSITIONS.  The
// three knobs already let an app do parallax by recomputing every node each frame
// — and that is an O(N) rewrite per scrolled frame AND a full instance re-upload,
// which is the retained tree's whole budget spent on standing still.  As an offset
// per run it costs one pair of floats.
//
// ⚠ AND IT TRANSLATES, IT DOES NOT SCALE.  Scaling by parallax is a perspective
// projection and would resize the footprint a base-anchored sprite is aligned to.
// Distance-as-smaller is the depth CUE's job (`depth_cue`), which scales about the
// origin and therefore keeps the feet planted; parallax only moves things.

// Move the camera.  Does NOT dirty the instance buffer — that is the point.
//
// An unsplit stage IS a one-view stage, so this is view 0's camera rather than a
// second place a camera can live — the same move P2 made with parallax, where
// flat scrolling is every factor at 1.0 instead of a separate path.
pub fn set_camera(self: Stage, x: float, y: float) { set_view_camera(self, 0, x, y); }

// Where the camera stands.  An unsplit stage IS a one-view stage, so this is
// view 0 rather than a second place a camera can live.
pub fn camera_x(self: const Stage) -> float { view_camera_x(self, 0) }
// The other half of the same pair.
pub fn camera_y(self: const Stage) -> float { view_camera_y(self, 0) }

// How much of the camera a layer feels.  1.0 is the world; below that is a
// background that lags; above it a foreground that leads.  Flat scrolling is every
// factor at 1.0, which is the degenerate case rather than a second code path.
pub fn set_parallax(self: Stage, layer: integer, factor: float) {
    grow_layers(self, layer);
    if layer < 0 { return; }
    self.st_layers[layer].ly_parallax = factor;
}

// What a layer's factor is now.  A layer nobody configured reads 1.0 — the
// world's own rate — so asking about one is always safe.
pub fn parallax_of(self: const Stage, layer: integer) -> float {
    layer_at(self, layer).ly_parallax
}

// Make sure `st_layers` reaches `layer`, filling with the plain layer.
fn grow_layers(self: Stage, layer: integer) {
    if layer < 0 { return; }
    while len(self.st_layers) <= layer {
        self.st_layers += [Layer { ly_parallax: 1.0, ly_fog: 0, ly_density: 0.0,
                                   ly_blur: 0 }];
    }
}

// ⚠ ALWAYS A FRESH RECORD, on every path.  Answering `self.st_layers[layer]`
// directly would be a borrow on one branch and a literal on the other — the
// mixed verdict that corrupted the store on `--native` in P6 (loft#1017).
fn layer_at(self: const Stage, layer: integer) -> Layer {
    if layer < 0 or layer >= len(self.st_layers) {
        return Layer { ly_parallax: 1.0, ly_fog: 0, ly_density: 0.0, ly_blur: 0 };
    }
    l = self.st_layers[layer] ?? Layer { ly_parallax: 1.0, ly_fog: 0,
                                         ly_density: 0.0, ly_blur: 0 };
    Layer { ly_parallax: l.ly_parallax, ly_fog: l.ly_fog, ly_density: l.ly_density,
            ly_blur: l.ly_blur }
}

// The screen-space offset a layer's content is drawn at, in view 0.
pub fn cam_offset(self: const Stage, layer: integer) -> P2 {
    view_offset(self, 0, layer)
}

// How many times the instance buffer has been written.  A pure camera move must
// not move this — the property the whole design turns on.
pub fn uploads(self: const Stage) -> integer { self.st_uploads }


// ── P3: ambient motion ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// ⚠ NOTHING PER FRAME AT ALL.  A field of grass, a canopy, a banner: the phase is
// derived from POSITION and the animation from a single clock, so five hundred
// swaying tufts are one buffer uploaded once and one uniform that changes.  This is
// the limit of what `P4`'s frame-index attribute exists to keep cheap — there, one
// integer per animating sprite; here, zero.
//
// ⚠ AND IT IS VISUAL ONLY.  Sway displaces what is DRAWN and never the footprint:
// a swaying tree keeps its depth, its bounds and its hit area, because a sprite
// that re-sorted as it swayed would flicker past its neighbours, and grass that
// dodged the pointer would be worse than grass that ignored it.

// Example: @STG-008 — ambient motion is free, out of step, and visual only.
// Advance the ambient clock.  Does NOT dirty the instance buffer — that is the
// whole point of deriving the phase rather than stepping it.
pub fn set_time(self: Stage, t: float) { self.st_time = t; }

// The ambient clock, which is the `t` every sway phase is derived from.
pub fn time_of(self: const Stage) -> float { self.st_time }

// How far node `idx` is displaced right now, in pixels.  Bounded by its amplitude
// by construction, since it is an amplitude times a sine.
pub fn sway_of(self: const Stage, idx: integer) -> float {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return 0.0; }
    n = self.st_nodes[idx];
    if n.nd_sway == 0.0 { return 0.0; }
    n.nd_sway * sin(self.st_time + n.nd_phase)
}


// ── P4: animation ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// ⚠ ADVANCED FROM A STEP, NEVER FROM WALL TIME.  `advance` takes the simulation's
// own delta, so a cycle is identical at any frame rate and replayable under a
// recorded input stream — the property co-op determinism rests on.  A clock read
// inside the library would quietly break both.
//
// ⚠ AND THE DELTA IS INTEGER MICROSECONDS, not seconds.  Accumulating a float
// drifts: `0.1` added eight times is `0.7999…`, which truncates to frame 7 where 8
// is right, and two 1/60 steps do not equal one 1/30 step. Both failures showed up
// the first time this was gated. Integer units are what `fixstep` speaks and what
// makes "the same total elapsed gives the same frame" true rather than nearly true.

// Register a sequence and answer its index, for `Place.pl_seq`.
pub fn add_sequence(self: Stage, first: integer, count: integer, fps: float,
                    mode: integer) -> integer {
    push_sequence(self, "", first, count, fps, mode)
}

// The one place a `Sequence` is built, so its name arrives with the rest of it
// and cannot be attached — or forgotten — afterwards.
fn push_sequence(self: Stage, name: text, first: integer, count: integer,
                 fps: float, mode: integer) -> integer {
    self.st_seqs += [Sequence { q_first: first,
                                q_count: if count < 1 { 1 } else { count },
                                q_fps: fps, q_mode: mode, q_name: name }];
    len(self.st_seqs) - 1
}

// Step every animated node by `dt_us` MICROSECONDS — the simulation's own delta,
// not a clock this library reads.
//
// ⚠ Dirties the instance buffer, unlike the camera and the ambient clock — an
// animated sprite genuinely changes one number per frame.  That number is a FRAME
// INDEX and not a uv rect, so it is one float and not four.
pub fn advance(self: Stage, dt_us: integer) {
    moved = false;
    for i in 0..len(self.st_nodes) {
        if self.st_nodes[i].nd_seq >= 0 {
            self.st_nodes[i].nd_elapsed = self.st_nodes[i].nd_elapsed + dt_us;
            moved = true;
        }
    }
    // ⚠ The property tweens ride THIS call rather than one of their own, which is
    // what stops a scene from owning two clocks — see § C2 below.
    if step_tweens(self, dt_us) { moved = true; }
    if moved { self.st_dirty = true; }
}

// Which cell node `idx` shows right now.
pub fn frame_of(self: const Stage, idx: integer) -> integer {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return 0; }
    n = self.st_nodes[idx];
    if n.nd_seq < 0 or n.nd_seq >= len(self.st_seqs) { return 0; }
    // ⚠ Indexed, not scanned.  `pack_instances` asks this once per node per
    // frame, so searching the sequence table would put the number of SEQUENCES
    // inside the loop over SPRITES — the per-frame cost this phase's design call
    // exists to hold at one number per sprite.  The bounds test above is what
    // makes the fallback unreachable.
    // ⚠ The fallback is a LITERAL, not a call.  A call in this position leaks the
    // record it returns on an index miss (loft#1013, both backends), and the
    // compiler's refusal of a struct-valued constant prescribes exactly that
    // leaking form.  A literal is the shape that does not.  It stays unreachable:
    // the bounds test above is what decides that, not this line.
    q = self.st_seqs[n.nd_seq] ?? Sequence { q_first: 0, q_count: 1, q_fps: 0.0,
                                             q_mode: LOOP, q_name: "" };
    raw = ((n.nd_elapsed as float) * q.q_fps / 1000000.0) as integer;
    if raw < 0 { raw = 0; }
    if q.q_count <= 1 { return q.q_first; }
    if q.q_mode == ONCE {
        if raw > q.q_count - 1 { raw = q.q_count - 1; }
        return q.q_first + raw;
    }
    if q.q_mode == PINGPONG {
        // ⚠ The period is 2n-2, NOT 2n: the two END frames are each shown once
        // per cycle, not twice.  A period of 2n stutters at both ends, which is
        // the classic off-by-one in a ping-pong and reads as a limp.
        period = q.q_count * 2 - 2;
        pp = raw % period ?? 0;
        if pp < q.q_count { return q.q_first + pp; }
        return q.q_first + period - pp;
    }
    q.q_first + (raw % q.q_count ?? 0)
}

// Reset a node to the start of its sequence.
pub fn restart(self: Stage, idx: integer) {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return; }
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_elapsed = 0;
    self.st_dirty = true;
}


// ── C2: a node's numbers, read, written, and moved over time ────────────────
//
// ⚠⚠ **THE WRITE SWITCH IS THE POINT, AND THERE IS EXACTLY ONE OF IT.**  loft has
// no property references, so "tween `node.x`" cannot be a pointer to a field — it
// has to be a name the library switches on.  `write_prop` is that switch, and
// both doors go through it: `set_prop` writes now, a tween writes every frame.
// Two switches would be two spellings of *what X means* that can disagree.
//
// ⚠ **`Prop.X` is the first public way to MOVE a node at all.**  Before this the
// stage could place a node and never shift it — a consumer had to reach into
// `st_nodes[i].nd_x` itself, which is a field write no gate could see.
//
// ⚠⚠ **AT MOST ONE TWEEN PER (NODE, PROPERTY), and starting another REPLACES it.**
// That is the invariant this section exists to hold, and it is structural rather
// than checked: two tweens on one property are two writers of one number, and the
// answer would depend on which happened to be later in a vector.  Replacement is
// also what a caller means — "now go there instead" — so the safe rule and the
// expected one are the same rule.
//
// ⚠ **A finished tween stops writing.**  It stays in the table so
// `prop_tweening` can answer, but it no longer touches the node — otherwise it
// would keep re-asserting its end value every frame and silently undo a later
// `set_prop`, which is the same two-writers defect one move down the line.
//
// ⚠ **The unit is the one `advance` already takes: MICROSECONDS.**  A duration
// here is `dt_us`, not `tween`'s own unit-agnostic count and not `fixstep`'s
// 3 000 000-per-second base — because the tween rides `advance`, and a scene that
// had to convert between two units to move a sprite would be carrying two clocks.
// A consumer on `fixstep` converts once, where it already converts for `advance`.

// Which of a node's numbers a tween drives.
//
// ⚠ Every one is a `float` the node already stores.  Colour, atlas and the
// sequence are not here on purpose: they are not numbers you can be *half way*
// between, and interpolating them would produce values the node never holds.
pub enum Prop {
    X, Y,
    Rot,
    ScaleX, ScaleY,
    OriginX, OriginY,
    Alpha,
    Depth,
}

// One property of one node, moving.
struct NodeTween {
    nt_node: integer,
    nt_prop: Prop = Prop.X,
    nt_tw: Tween,
}

// What that property reads right now.
pub fn prop_of(self: const Stage, idx: integer, p: Prop) -> float {
    if idx >= 0 {
        if idx < len(self.st_nodes) {
            n = self.st_nodes[idx];
            return match p {
                X       => n.nd_x,
                Y       => n.nd_y,
                Rot     => n.nd_rot,
                ScaleX  => n.nd_sx,
                ScaleY  => n.nd_sy,
                OriginX => n.nd_ox,
                OriginY => n.nd_oy,
                Alpha   => n.nd_alpha,
                Depth   => n.nd_depth,
            };
        }
    }
    0.0
}

// Write it now.  The immediate door — the tween door writes through the same
// switch, one frame at a time.
pub fn set_prop(self: Stage, idx: integer, p: Prop, v: float) {
    write_prop(self, idx, p, v);
    self.st_dirty = true;
}

// THE SWITCH.  Every write to a node's number goes through here.
fn write_prop(self: Stage, idx: integer, p: Prop, v: float) {
    if idx >= 0 {
        if idx < len(self.st_nodes) {
            match p {
                X       => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_x = v,
                Y       => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_y = v,
                Rot     => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_rot = v,
                ScaleX  => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_sx = v,
                ScaleY  => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_sy = v,
                OriginX => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_ox = v,
                OriginY => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_oy = v,
                Alpha   => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_alpha = v,
                Depth   => self.st_nodes[idx].nd_depth = v,
            }
        }
    }
}

// Move `p` from where it is now to `to`, over `dur_us` microseconds.
//
// ⚠ `from` is read at THIS moment, so a tween started mid-flight carries on from
// where the last one had got to rather than snapping back to a remembered start.
// Replaces any tween already on this (node, property).
pub fn tween_prop(self: Stage, idx: integer, p: Prop, to: float, dur_us: integer,
                  c: Curve = Curve.Linear,
                  s: Shape = Shape.In) {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return; }
    cancel_prop_tween(self, idx, p);
    self.st_tweens += [NodeTween {
        nt_node: idx, nt_prop: p,
        nt_tw: tween_new(prop_of(self, idx, p), to, dur_us, c, s),
    }];
}

// Is that property still moving?
pub fn prop_tweening(self: const Stage, idx: integer, p: Prop) -> boolean {
    for t in self.st_tweens {
        if t.nt_node == idx and t.nt_prop == p and !t.nt_tw.done() { return true; }
    }
    false
}

// Stop it wherever it has got to.  The property keeps its current value.
pub fn cancel_prop_tween(self: Stage, idx: integer, p: Prop) {
    kept: vector<NodeTween> = [];
    for t in self.st_tweens {
        if t.nt_node != idx or t.nt_prop != p { kept += [t]; }
    }
    self.st_tweens = kept;
}

// Drop every tween on a node.
pub fn cancel_node_tweens(self: Stage, idx: integer) {
    kept_n: vector<NodeTween> = [];
    for t in self.st_tweens {
        if t.nt_node != idx { kept_n += [t]; }
    }
    self.st_tweens = kept_n;
}

// How many property tweens the stage is holding — the gate reads this.
pub fn tween_count(self: const Stage) -> integer { len(self.st_tweens) }

// Step every property tween and write what it now reads.  Answers whether any of
// them moved, so `advance` can dirty the buffer exactly when something did.
fn step_tweens(self: Stage, dt_us: integer) -> boolean {
    if dt_us <= 0 { return false; }
    any = false;
    for i in 0..len(self.st_tweens) {
        if i < len(self.st_tweens) {
            if !self.st_tweens[i].nt_tw.done() {
                _ = self.st_tweens[i].nt_tw.advance(dt_us);
                write_prop(self, self.st_tweens[i].nt_node,
                           self.st_tweens[i].nt_prop,
                           self.st_tweens[i].nt_tw.value());
                any = true;
            }
        }
    }
    any
}


// ── P5: facings, and the two models the projection picks between ─────────────
//
// ⚠ THE PROJECTION PICKS THE MODEL, NOT THE AUTHOR.  An author writes one call —
// `face(node, angle)`, "this thing points that way" — and the stage decides what
// that costs.  A top-down world turns one sprite continuously; a side-on one
// cannot turn a standing sprite at all, so it picks from an (action, facing)
// table and mirrors at most.  Putting the choice in the projection is what lets
// the same mob code serve both views, which is the claim the 2.5-D vehicle rests
// on.

// One sprite authored in a locked orientation, rotated continuously to the
// facing.  Pre-rotated frames never exist, so 15-degree steps cost no cells.
pub const TOP_DOWN: integer = 0;
// A standing sprite cannot be rotated into another facing — at most mirrored —
// so the facing selects a sequence and the other side is a flip.
pub const SIDE_ON: integer = 1;

// Pick `TOP_DOWN` or `SIDE_ON`.  It decides what `face` does with an angle:
// rotate the one sprite, or resolve a sequence and mirror it.
pub fn set_projection(self: Stage, mode: integer) { self.st_proj = mode; }

// Which of the two models is in force.
pub fn projection(self: const Stage) -> integer { self.st_proj }

// How many sequences are registered; also the bound on a `pl_seq` index.
pub fn sequence_count(self: const Stage) -> integer { len(self.st_seqs) }

// Register a sequence under a NAME, so `face` and `set_action` can resolve it.
// The convention is `<key>`, `<key>_<action>`, `<key>_<action>_<facing>`, and
// resolution walks it most-specific first.
pub fn add_named_sequence(self: Stage, name: text, first: integer, count: integer,
                          fps: float, mode: integer) -> integer {
    push_sequence(self, name, first, count, fps, mode)
}

// The sequence registered under `name`, or -1.  Names are resolved when a facing
// or an action CHANGES, never per frame, which is what keeps a scan honest here.
fn seq_named(self: const Stage, name: text) -> integer {
    if name == "" { return -1; }
    found = -1;
    for i in 0..len(self.st_seqs) {
        qi = self.st_seqs[i];
        if qi.q_name == name { found = i; }
    }
    found
}

// Which of the four compass facings an angle points at.
//
// ⚠ SCREEN SPACE, Y DOWN: angle 0 is +x (east) and +pi/2 is +y (south), matching
// `pl_rot` and the shader.  The edges are HALF-OPEN, as A4's picking rects are —
// 45 degrees belongs to south — so no angle is claimed by two facings.
pub fn facing_name(angle: float) -> text {
    turn = PI * 2.0;
    norm = angle - floor(angle / turn ?? 0.0) * turn;
    b = ((norm + PI / 4.0) / (PI / 2.0) ?? 0.0) as integer;
    q = b % 4 ?? 0;
    if q == 0 { return "e"; }
    if q == 1 { return "s"; }
    if q == 2 { return "w"; }
    "n"
}

// The facing a flip would show instead, or "" where a flip shows nothing new —
// a front view mirrored is still a front view, so north and south have no partner.
fn opposite_facing(f: text) -> text {
    if f == "e" { return "w"; }
    if f == "w" { return "e"; }
    ""
}

// Resolve a node's sequence from its key, action and facing, and say whether the
// answer has to be mirrored to face the right way.
//
// ⚠ FALL BACK, NEVER FAIL.  A missing sprite leaves the mob drawn with what it
// already had — the rule crawler's `<key>.png` loader keeps, and the reason a
// half-authored mob is a visibly wrong sprite rather than a blank or a stop.
fn resolve_facing(self: Stage, idx: integer) {
    // An index that is not a node reads as the blank node, whose `nd_key` is
    // empty — so the next line returns, which is the fall-back this documents.
    n = self.st_nodes[idx]?;
    if n.nd_key == "" { return; }
    f = facing_name(n.nd_facing);
    mirror = false;
    q = -1;
    if n.nd_action != "" {
        q = seq_named(self, "{n.nd_key}_{n.nd_action}_{f}");
        if q < 0 {
            // At most mirrored: borrow the other side rather than turning.
            opp = opposite_facing(f);
            if opp != "" {
                q = seq_named(self, "{n.nd_key}_{n.nd_action}_{opp}");
                if q >= 0 { mirror = true; }
            }
        }
        if q < 0 { q = seq_named(self, "{n.nd_key}_{n.nd_action}"); }
    }
    if q < 0 { q = seq_named(self, "{n.nd_key}_{f}"); }
    if q < 0 { q = seq_named(self, n.nd_key); }
    if q < 0 { return; }               // nothing matched: keep what it had
    // ⚠ THE PHASE CARRIES OVER — `nd_elapsed` is deliberately not touched.  A mob
    // turning a corner mid-stride must keep walking from where its legs were; a
    // reset here is a visible stutter at the exact moment a player is watching.
    // `restart` stays the one way to go back to frame 0.
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_seq = q;
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_mirror = mirror;
    self.st_dirty = true;
}

// Point a node at `angle` radians.  Top-down turns it; side-on picks and flips.
pub fn face(self: Stage, idx: integer, angle: float) {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return; }
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_facing = angle;
    if self.st_proj == TOP_DOWN {
        // Continuous, off the 2x3 affine A1 already composes — so a finer step
        // costs arithmetic and never an atlas entry.
        self.st_nodes[idx].nd_rot = angle;
        self.st_nodes[idx].nd_mirror = false;
        self.st_dirty = true;
        return;
    }
    resolve_facing(self, idx);
}

// What a node is doing — "idle", "walk", "attack".  Re-resolves, phase intact.
pub fn set_action(self: Stage, idx: integer, action: text) {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return; }
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_action = action;
    if self.st_proj == SIDE_ON { resolve_facing(self, idx); }
}

// Where the node points, in radians.  Kept in BOTH models — side-on holds the
// angle even though what it draws for it is a chosen sequence and a flip.
pub fn facing_of(self: const Stage, idx: integer) -> float {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return 0.0; }
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_facing
}

// What the node is doing, as `set_action` last left it.  Name resolution reads
// this together with the key and the facing.
pub fn action_of(self: const Stage, idx: integer) -> text {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return ""; }
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_action
}

// The sprite's own rotation, which side-on leaves alone.
pub fn rotation_of(self: const Stage, idx: integer) -> float {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return 0.0; }
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_rot
}

// Is this node drawn flipped?  Only side-on ever sets it: mirroring is how the
// other half of the facings exists without another atlas cell.
pub fn mirrored(self: const Stage, idx: integer) -> boolean {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return false; }
    self.st_nodes[idx].nd_mirror
}


// ── P6: several views over one stage ─────────────────────────────────────────
//
// ⚠ ONE SCENE, PACKED ONCE, DRAWN N TIMES.  A view carries only a camera and a
// screen rect, so adding one costs a draw pass and never a re-pack — which is
// P2's *the camera is a uniform* carried to N cameras.  A view that baked its
// camera into node positions would spend A3's entire budget on looking twice.
//
// ⚠ AND EVERY VIEW SHARES ONE SCREEN-SPACE PROJECTION.  A view is placed by
// OFFSETTING into its rect and scissoring to it, not by remapping the clip
// volume: the software path and the GL path then stay directly comparable — the
// parity rig A2 and A3b rest on — and a sprite is the same size in every view
// rather than quietly zoomed by a viewport of a different shape.

pub fn view_count(self: const Stage) -> integer { len(self.st_views) }

// Add a view painting into the screen rect `(x, y, w, h)`.  A width of 0 or less
// means unbounded — the whole target, which is what view 0 starts as.
pub fn add_view(self: Stage, x: integer, y: integer, w: integer,
                h: integer) -> integer {
    self.st_views += [View { vw_cam_x: 0.0, vw_cam_y: 0.0, vw_x: x, vw_y: y,
                             vw_w: w, vw_h: h }];
    len(self.st_views) - 1
}

// Move or resize a view's rectangle on screen.  The camera it carries is left
// alone, so a split-screen resize does not move what either half is looking at.
pub fn set_view_rect(self: Stage, view: integer, x: integer, y: integer,
                     w: integer, h: integer) {
    if view < 0 or view >= len(self.st_views) { return; }
    self.st_views[view].vw_x = x;
    self.st_views[view].vw_y = y;
    self.st_views[view].vw_w = w;
    self.st_views[view].vw_h = h;
}

// Move one view's camera.  Like `set_camera`, it does not dirty the buffer.
pub fn set_view_camera(self: Stage, view: integer, x: float, y: float) {
    if view < 0 or view >= len(self.st_views) { return; }
    self.st_views[view].vw_cam_x = x;
    self.st_views[view].vw_cam_y = y;
}

fn view_at(self: const Stage, view: integer) -> View {
    if view < 0 or view >= len(self.st_views) {
        return View { vw_cam_x: 0.0, vw_cam_y: 0.0, vw_x: 0, vw_y: 0,
                      vw_w: 0, vw_h: 0 };
    }
    // ⚠ ONE OWNERSHIP VERDICT ON EVERY PATH.  Answering `self.st_views[view]`
    // directly is a BORROW, while the guard above answers a FRESH record — one
    // function telling its caller two different things about who frees the
    // result — and on `--native` that corrupted the store several calls later,
    // reading canvas pixels as a record number (loft#1017).  Copying the fields
    // out makes every return fresh.
    v = self.st_views[view] ?? View { vw_cam_x: 0.0, vw_cam_y: 0.0, vw_x: 0,
                                      vw_y: 0, vw_w: 0, vw_h: 0 };
    View { vw_cam_x: v.vw_cam_x, vw_cam_y: v.vw_cam_y, vw_x: v.vw_x,
           vw_y: v.vw_y, vw_w: v.vw_w, vw_h: v.vw_h }
}

// Where this view's camera stands.  A view out of range answers 0.0 rather than
// failing, which is `view_at`'s verdict and not a second rule.
pub fn view_camera_x(self: const Stage, view: integer) -> float {
    view_at(self, view).vw_cam_x
}

// The other half of the same pair.
pub fn view_camera_y(self: const Stage, view: integer) -> float {
    view_at(self, view).vw_cam_y
}

// Where a layer's content lands on screen in this view: the camera un-applied by
// the layer's parallax, then moved into the view's rect.
//
// ⚠ THE VIEWPORT ORIGIN IS PART OF THE MAPPING, not decoration.  Leave it out and
// both views draw on top of each other while every camera test still passes.
pub fn view_offset(self: const Stage, view: integer, layer: integer) -> P2 {
    v = view_at(self, view);
    f = parallax_of(self, layer);
    P2 { p_x: -v.vw_cam_x * f + (v.vw_x as float),
         p_y: -v.vw_cam_y * f + (v.vw_y as float) }
}

// Pack the instance buffer if the scene changed, and say whether it did.
//
// One home for *when does the buffer get rewritten*, so the GL path and any
// count of uploads cannot disagree about it — and so N views can be shown to
// cost ONE upload without a GL context to prove it in.
pub fn sync_instances(self: Stage) -> boolean {
    if !self.st_dirty { return false; }
    self.st_uploads = self.st_uploads + 1;
    self.st_dirty = false;
    true
}

// The topmost node under a screen point AS THAT VIEW SEES IT, or -1.
//
// ⚠ ONE SCREEN POINT IS A DIFFERENT WORLD POINT IN EACH VIEW — the same reason
// P2 un-applies the camera per LAYER.  A point outside the view's own rect is
// not that view's to answer, whatever its camera would map it to.
pub fn pick_in(self: const Stage, view: integer, sx: float, sy: float) -> integer {
    if !in_view_rect(self, view, sx, sy) { return -1; }
    k = len(self.st_order) - 1;
    while k >= 0 {
        i = self.st_order[k] ?? -1;
        if hits_in(self, view, i, sx, sy) { return i; }
        k = k - 1;
    }
    -1
}

// Is this screen point inside the view's rect?  An unbounded view holds every
// point, which is what keeps the unsplit stage a single code path.
pub fn in_view_rect(self: const Stage, view: integer, sx: float, sy: float) -> boolean {
    v = view_at(self, view);
    if v.vw_w <= 0 or v.vw_h <= 0 { return true; }
    // Half-open, as A4's picking rects are, so abutting views never both claim
    // a pixel — the seam of a split screen is exactly where that would show.
    sx >= (v.vw_x as float) and sy >= (v.vw_y as float)
        and sx < ((v.vw_x + v.vw_w) as float)
        and sy < ((v.vw_y + v.vw_h) as float)
}

// Does this node cover the screen point, as `view` maps it?
pub fn hits_in(self: const Stage, view: integer, idx: integer, sx: float,
               sy: float) -> boolean {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return false; }
    n = self.st_nodes[idx];
    if !n.nd_visible or n.nd_w <= 0.0 or n.nd_h <= 0.0 { return false; }
    off = view_offset(self, view, n.nd_layer);
    l = to_local(self, idx, sx - off.p_x, sy - off.p_y);
    if l.p_x < 0.0 or l.p_y < 0.0 or l.p_x >= n.nd_w or l.p_y >= n.nd_h { return false; }
    solid_at(self, idx, l.p_x, l.p_y)
}


// ── L1: light sampled per sprite, applied as tint ────────────────────────────
//
// ⚠ NO PASS, NO FRAMEBUFFER, NO REORDER.  Each sprite samples every light at its
// own ORIGIN and the answer folds into A5's tint attribute, so lighting a scene
// costs a multiply per sprite and cannot disturb the draw.  That makes it
// order-independent by construction, which is the property L2's composite has to
// work for rather than inherit.
//
// ⚠ AND THE SAMPLE POINT IS THE ORIGIN — the footprint P1 sorts on, not the
// artwork.  Two mobs standing on one tile take the same light however tall they
// are; sampling a corner would light a tall sprite as though it stood further
// away, which is the 2.5-D wrong picture wearing a different hat.

// Add a light.  Answers its index, for `move_light`.
pub fn add_light(self: Stage, x: float, y: float, radius: float, colour: integer,
                 power: float) -> integer {
    self.st_lights += [StageLight { lt_x: x, lt_y: y, lt_r: radius, lt_colour: colour,
                               lt_power: power }];
    // ⚠ Unlike a camera, a light DIRTIES the buffer: it changes what is in the
    // instance data rather than how it is looked at.  A moving torch therefore
    // re-packs every frame, and taking that cost off the per-sprite path is
    // exactly what L2's composite pass is for.
    self.st_dirty = true;
    len(self.st_lights) - 1
}

// Carry a light to a new place.  DIRTIES the buffer, exactly as adding one does
// — a moving torch therefore re-packs every frame, which is the cost L2's
// composite pass exists to take off the per-sprite path.
pub fn move_light(self: Stage, i: integer, x: float, y: float) {
    if i < 0 or i >= len(self.st_lights) { return; }
    self.st_lights[i].lt_x = x;
    self.st_lights[i].lt_y = y;
    self.st_dirty = true;
}

// How many lights the scene holds; the bound for `move_light`.
pub fn light_count(self: const Stage) -> integer { len(self.st_lights) }

// How lit the world is before any light: 1.0 leaves the material untouched, so a
// game that never asks for light cannot be dimmed by this existing.  Turn it
// down and the scene goes dark except where a light reaches.
pub fn set_ambient(self: Stage, level: float) {
    self.st_ambient = level;
    self.st_dirty = true;
}

// The level `set_ambient` left.
pub fn ambient(self: const Stage) -> float { self.st_ambient }

// What a light contributes at a point: linear in the radius, clamped at both ends.
//
// ⚠ LINEAR, `1 - d/r`, and that is a design call.  This phase's gate is
// hand-computed values, and a curve nobody can hand-compute is a gate nobody
// maintains.  L2 owns the prettier curve, and its own gate says how: expectations
// GENERATED from the same constants the shader uses, so retuning is one edit.
pub fn light_reach(l: const StageLight, px: float, py: float) -> float {
    if l.lt_r <= 0.0 { return 0.0; }
    dx = px - l.lt_x;
    dy = py - l.lt_y;
    d = sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy);
    a = 1.0 - (d / l.lt_r ?? 1.0);
    if a <= 0.0 { return 0.0; }
    if a > 1.0 { return 1.0; }
    // ⚠ ONE HOME FOR THE CURVE.  L2's composite shader takes this same exponent
    // as a uniform rather than baking a number into its text, so retuning the
    // falloff is one edit and the per-sprite and per-pixel paths cannot drift.
    if LIGHT_FALLOFF != 1.0 { a = pow(a, LIGHT_FALLOFF) ?? 0.0; }
    a * l.lt_power
}

// The colour a node is actually drawn in: its material, multiplied by the light
// standing on its footprint.
//
// ⚠ ONE HOME.  Both render paths and the instance packer read this rather than
// each folding the light in themselves — the shape P6 shipped a defect through,
// where one fact derived per path was validated only where the derivations
// happened to agree.
pub fn lit_colour(self: const Stage, idx: integer) -> integer {
    if idx < 0 or idx >= len(self.st_nodes) { return 0; }
    n = self.st_nodes[idx];
    // ⚠ ONE LIGHTING MODEL AT A TIME.  With the map on, the composite lights
    // every pixel — so the sprite goes in RAW.  Tinting it here as well would
    // light it twice, and a torch-lit mob would come out darker than the floor
    // it stands on.
    if self.st_light_map { return n.nd_colour; }
    if len(self.st_lights) == 0 and self.st_ambient >= 1.0 { return n.nd_colour; }
    o = world_origin(self, idx);
    lv = light_rgb_at(self, o.p_x, o.p_y);
    lr = lv.li_r;
    lg = lv.li_g;
    lb = lv.li_b;
    // ⚠ CLAMPED PER CHANNEL.  Unclamped, standing between two torches multiplies
    // past 255 and the byte wraps, so the brightest place in the scene comes out
    // DARK — the classic and the one everybody finds by accident.
    pack_channel(n.nd_colour >> 16, lr) << 16
        | pack_channel(n.nd_colour >> 8, lg) << 8
        | pack_channel(n.nd_colour, lb)
}

fn pack_channel(material: integer, level: float) -> integer {
    k = level;
    if k < 0.0 { k = 0.0; }
    if k > 1.0 { k = 1.0; }
    v = (((material & 255) as float) * k + 0.5) as integer;
    if v < 0 { return 0; }
    if v > 255 { return 255; }
    v
}


// ── L2: the light-map composite pass ─────────────────────────────────────────
//
// What L1 cannot do: light that falls ACROSS the scene rather than across the
// sprites.  The world draws, ONE multiply composite lights every pixel of it —
// including the gaps no sprite covers, which is why it earns a pass at all — and
// the HUD draws after, unlit.
//
// ⚠ A MULTIPLY AFTER THE SCENE IS ORDER-INDEPENDENT, so this does not fight
// A3a's *never reorder*: the composite cannot care which sprite was drawn first.
//
// ⚠ AND VISIBILITY STAYS THE APP'S.  The light PRESENTS; it does not decide what
// is seen.  A pixel in the dark is still there, still picked, still in its batch.

// The exponent on `1 - d/r`.  One home, read by `light_reach` and handed to the
// composite shader as a uniform — never baked into its text, both because a
// second copy drifts and because loft renders `2.0` as `2`, which GLSL takes as
// an int and refuses.
pub const LIGHT_FALLOFF: float = 1.0;

// The sentinel meaning "no HUD band": every layer is world.
pub const HUD_NONE: integer = 1000000;

// A light level, per channel, 0..1.
pub struct Lit {
    li_r: float,
    li_g: float,
    li_b: float,
}

// Switch the composite pass on.  Off, light is L1's per-sprite tint, which is
// one colour for a whole sprite; on, it is resolved per pixel.
pub fn set_light_map(self: Stage, on: boolean) {
    self.st_light_map = on;
    self.st_dirty = true;
}

// Is the composite pass on?
pub fn light_map(self: const Stage) -> boolean { self.st_light_map }

// Layers at or above `n` draw AFTER the composite and take no light.
pub fn set_hud_layer(self: Stage, n: integer) { self.st_hud_layer = n; }

// The first layer that draws after the composite, or `HUD_NONE`.
pub fn hud_layer(self: const Stage) -> integer { self.st_hud_layer }

// The light standing at a world point, per channel.
//
// ⚠ THE ONE HOME both samplings read: L1 asks it at a sprite's origin, L2 asks
// it per pixel.  One curve, one floor, one clamp — so the per-sprite and the
// per-pixel answers cannot disagree about the same point.
pub fn light_rgb_at(self: const Stage, wx: float, wy: float) -> Lit {
    lr = self.st_ambient;
    lg = self.st_ambient;
    lb = self.st_ambient;
    for i in 0..len(self.st_lights) {
        l = self.st_lights[i];
        a = light_reach(l, wx, wy);
        // Skipping zero is work avoided, NOT the guard: `light_reach` is the one
        // place that decides a light out of range contributes nothing, and it is
        // gated there.  Two places enforcing one rule is how they drift.
        if a <= 0.0 { continue; }
        lr = lr + (((l.lt_colour >> 16) & 255) as float / 255.0) * a;
        lg = lg + (((l.lt_colour >> 8) & 255) as float / 255.0) * a;
        lb = lb + ((l.lt_colour & 255) as float / 255.0) * a;
    }
    Lit { li_r: clamp01(lr), li_g: clamp01(lg), li_b: clamp01(lb) }
}

// The scalar level at a world point — the green channel of a white light, and
// the number the monotonic-and-floored invariant is stated about.
pub fn light_level_at(self: const Stage, wx: float, wy: float) -> float {
    light_rgb_at(self, wx, wy).li_g
}

fn clamp01(v: float) -> float {
    if v < 0.0 { return 0.0; }
    if v > 1.0 { return 1.0; }
    v
}

// Multiply every pixel of the view's rect by the light standing there.
//
// ⚠ THE SCREEN IS MAPPED TO THE WORLD THROUGH THE WORLD PLANE — layer 0, the
// plane lights live on.  A parallaxed background is therefore lit as though it
// stood on that plane, which is the honest simplification here: per-layer
// atmosphere is L3's question, not this pass's.
pub fn composite_light(self: const Stage, view: integer, cv: graphics::Canvas) {
    if !self.st_light_map { return; }
    off = view_offset(self, view, 0);
    vp = view_at(self, view);
    x0 = 0;
    y0 = 0;
    x1 = cv.width;
    y1 = cv.height;
    if vp.vw_w > 0 and vp.vw_h > 0 {
        x0 = vp.vw_x;
        y0 = vp.vw_y;
        if vp.vw_x + vp.vw_w < x1 { x1 = vp.vw_x + vp.vw_w; }
        if vp.vw_y + vp.vw_h < y1 { y1 = vp.vw_y + vp.vw_h; }
    }
    if x0 < 0 { x0 = 0; }
    if y0 < 0 { y0 = 0; }
    for py in y0..y1 {
        for px in x0..x1 {
            lv = light_rgb_at(self, (px as float) - off.p_x, (py as float) - off.p_y);
            d = cv.get_pixel(px, py);
            cv.set_pixel(px, py, (d & 0xff000000)
                | (scale_channel((d >> 16) & 255, lv.li_r) << 16)
                | (scale_channel((d >> 8) & 255, lv.li_g) << 8)
                | scale_channel(d & 255, lv.li_b));
        }
    }
}

fn scale_channel(c: integer, level: float) -> integer {
    v = ((c as float) * level + 0.5) as integer;
    if v < 0 { return 0; }
    if v > 255 { return 255; }
    v
}

// The composite's fragment shader.  Public so a test can check it reads the
// falloff and the floor as UNIFORMS rather than carrying its own copy of them.
pub fn composite_shader_source() -> text { FRAG_LIGHT }


// ── L3: per-layer atmosphere — fog and blur ──────────────────────────────────
//
// A layer already carries a parallax factor (P2).  Give it a fog colour, a
// density and a blur radius, and *distant, hazy, out-of-focus* becomes LAYER
// DATA rather than an effects pipeline.
//
// ⚠ FOG IS FREE, BLUR IS NOT.  Fog is a lerp toward a colour, folded into the
// same per-node mix the depth cue's haze already uses, so it costs nothing and
// needs no buffer.  Blur has to see a layer's neighbouring pixels, so it is a
// pass over what has been drawn — which is why they are separate switches and
// why only one of them is worth thinking about per frame.

// Fade this layer toward `colour`.  Density 0 is clear and changes nothing.
pub fn set_layer_fog(self: Stage, layer: integer, colour: integer, density: float) {
    grow_layers(self, layer);
    if layer < 0 { return; }
    self.st_layers[layer].ly_fog = colour;
    self.st_layers[layer].ly_density = density;
    self.st_dirty = true;
}

// The colour this layer fades toward.  An unset layer reads the plain layer's,
// so a scene that never asked for fog cannot be tinted by this existing.
pub fn layer_fog_colour(self: const Stage, layer: integer) -> integer {
    layer_at(self, layer).ly_fog
}

// How far it fades: 0 is clear.
pub fn layer_fog_density(self: const Stage, layer: integer) -> float {
    layer_at(self, layer).ly_density
}

// Put this layer out of focus by `radius` pixels.  0 is sharp.
//
// ⚠ THE BLUR RUNS WHEN THE LAYER FINISHES, so it softens everything drawn up to
// and including it and NOTHING above — which is what makes a sharp foreground
// over a hazy distance possible, and what a fullscreen blur cannot do.  Distant
// bands therefore blur together, which is what *distance is out of focus* means.
pub fn set_layer_blur(self: Stage, layer: integer, radius: integer) {
    grow_layers(self, layer);
    if layer < 0 { return; }
    self.st_layers[layer].ly_blur = radius;
}

// This layer's blur radius in pixels; 0 is sharp.
pub fn layer_blur(self: const Stage, layer: integer) -> integer {
    layer_at(self, layer).ly_blur
}

// Box-blur a rectangle of the canvas in place.
//
// ⚠ SAMPLES ARE CLAMPED TO THE EDGE, never read as black.  Counting an
// off-image sample as black loses light everywhere the kernel overhangs, and it
// shows as a dark frame around the picture — the classic bug this phase gates
// against.  Clamping keeps a uniform field EXACTLY itself.
pub fn blur_region(cv: graphics::Canvas, x0: integer, y0: integer, x1: integer,
                   y1: integer, radius: integer) {
    // Returning early on 0 is work avoided, NOT the guard: a radius-0 kernel
    // averages exactly one sample and is already the identity.  What the gate
    // actually defends is that 0 means NO blur rather than the smallest one.
    if radius <= 0 { return; }
    w = x1 - x0;
    h = y1 - y0;
    if w <= 0 or h <= 0 { return; }
    src: vector<integer> = [];
    for py in y0..y1 {
        for px in x0..x1 { src += [cv.get_pixel(px, py)]; }
    }
    for py in 0..h {
        for px in 0..w {
            sr = 0;
            sg = 0;
            sb = 0;
            n = 0;
            for ky in (py - radius)..(py + radius + 1) {
                jy = ky;
                if jy < 0 { jy = 0; }
                if jy >= h { jy = h - 1; }
                for kx in (px - radius)..(px + radius + 1) {
                    jx = kx;
                    if jx < 0 { jx = 0; }
                    if jx >= w { jx = w - 1; }
                    v: integer = src[jy * w + jx] ?? 0;
                    sr = sr + ((v >> 16) & 255);
                    sg = sg + ((v >> 8) & 255);
                    sb = sb + (v & 255);
                    n = n + 1;
                }
            }
            cv.set_pixel(x0 + px, y0 + py, 0xff000000
                | (((sr + n / 2 ?? 0) / n ?? 0) << 16)
                | (((sg + n / 2 ?? 0) / n ?? 0) << 8)
                | ((sb + n / 2 ?? 0) / n ?? 0));
        }
    }
}

tests/ambient.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 P3 — ambient motion: a field of grass that costs nothing per frame.
//
// The phase comes from POSITION and the animation from one clock, so five hundred
// swaying tufts are a buffer uploaded once and a uniform that changes.  Everything
// below asserts that property rather than describing it.
use stage;
use graphics;

fn grass(x: float, sway: float) -> stage::Place {
  stage::Place { pl_x: x, pl_y: 10.0, pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 6.0, pl_sway: sway,
                 pl_colour: 0x00ff00 }
}

// ⚠ THE GATE: 500 sprites, 100 ticks of the clock, NOT ONE FLOAT of the instance
// buffer changed.  Stepping a stored phase would rewrite all 500 every frame.
fn test_five_hundred_swaying_sprites_cost_no_updates() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  for i in 0..500 { _ = st.node_add(-1, grass((i * 3) as float, 4.0)); }
  st.compose();
  before = st.pack_instances();
  for t in 0..100 { st.set_time((t as float) * 0.1); }
  after = st.pack_instances();
  assert(len(before) == len(after), "the buffer keeps its size");
  differs = 0;
  for i in 0..len(before) { if before[i] != after[i] { differs = differs + 1; } }
  assert(differs == 0, "{differs} floats changed across 100 ticks — expected 0");
}

// ⚠ AND THEY ARE NOT IN LOCKSTEP.  A phase derived from position is the whole
// reason a field looks like a field; one clock with no phase would sway every tuft
// identically, which reads as the ground moving rather than the grass.
fn test_neighbours_are_out_of_step() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, grass(0.0, 5.0));
  b = st.node_add(-1, grass(7.0, 5.0));
  c = st.node_add(-1, grass(19.0, 5.0));
  st.compose();
  st.set_time(0.3);
  sa = st.sway_of(a);
  sb = st.sway_of(b);
  sc = st.sway_of(c);
  assert(sa != sb and sb != sc and sa != sc,
         "three tufts, three displacements: {sa} {sb} {sc}");
}

// The clock moves them: the same tuft is somewhere else a moment later.
fn test_the_clock_moves_them() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, grass(4.0, 5.0));
  st.compose();
  st.set_time(0.0);
  at0 = st.sway_of(n);
  st.set_time(1.0);
  at1 = st.sway_of(n);
  assert(at0 != at1, "the displacement follows the clock");
}

// Amplitude bounds it — a sine times an amplitude can do nothing else, and a tuft
// that wandered off its tile would be a bug you only see in a wide shot.
fn test_the_sway_is_bounded_by_its_amplitude() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, grass(3.0, 2.0));
  st.compose();
  for t in 0..64 {
    st.set_time((t as float) * 0.25);
    v = st.sway_of(n);
    assert(v <= 2.0 and v >= -2.0, "displacement {v} escaped its amplitude");
  }
}

// Zero amplitude is still — the degenerate case proved, so a scene with no wind is
// the same picture it was before sway existed.
fn test_no_amplitude_is_no_motion() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, grass(3.0, 0.0));
  st.compose();
  st.set_time(0.0);
  a = graphics::canvas(32, 32, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(a);
  st.set_time(12.7);
  b = graphics::canvas(32, 32, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(b);
  assert(st.sway_of(n) == 0.0, "no displacement");
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { assert(a.data[i] == b.data[i], "and an identical picture"); }
}

// ⚠ SWAY IS VISUAL ONLY.  It displaces what is drawn and never the footprint, so a
// swaying tree keeps its depth, its bounds and its hit area — one that re-sorted as
// it swayed would flicker past its neighbours.
fn test_sway_does_not_move_the_footprint() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, grass(10.0, 6.0));
  st.compose();
  st.set_time(0.0);
  b0 = st.bounds_of(n);
  o0 = st.world_origin(n);
  hit0 = st.pick(11.0, 12.0);
  st.set_time(1.9);
  b1 = st.bounds_of(n);
  o1 = st.world_origin(n);
  assert(st.sway_of(n) != 0.0, "it IS swaying");
  assert(b0.b_x0 == b1.b_x0 and b0.b_x1 == b1.b_x1, "bounds do not move");
  assert(o0.p_x == o1.p_x, "the origin does not move");
  assert(st.pick(11.0, 12.0) == hit0, "and neither does what you can click");
}

// The picture does change, though — a sway that no one could see would pass every
// numeric test above.
fn test_the_drawn_picture_actually_moves() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, grass(10.0, 6.0));
  st.compose();
  st.set_time(0.0);
  a = graphics::canvas(48, 32, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(a);
  st.set_time(1.6);
  b = graphics::canvas(48, 32, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(b);
  moved = false;
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if a.data[i] != b.data[i] { moved = true; } }
  assert(moved, "the drawn grass moved between the two clocks");
}

tests/animation.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 P4 — mob animation.  Every expected frame is worked out by hand, and the
// three cells that matter are the ones that are wrong in most implementations:
// a loop that returns to 0 EXACTLY, two frame rates agreeing, and a ping-pong that
// does not stutter at its ends.
use stage;

fn walker(st: stage::Stage, seq: integer) -> integer {
  st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0, pl_seq: seq, pl_cols: 4 })
}

// A loop's worth of ticks returns to frame 0 exactly — not to 1, and not to the
// last frame.
fn test_a_loop_returns_to_frame_zero() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(0, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);   // 4 frames at 10 fps = 0.4 s
  n = walker(st, q);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 0, "starts on 0");
  st.advance(400000);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 0, "one full loop later, back on 0");
  st.advance(100000);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 1, "and moving again");
}

// ⚠ TICKED, NOT JUMPED — and this is the cell that pins integer microseconds.
// A game reaches 0.8 s by adding 0.1 s eight times, never by asking for 800000 at
// once, and in FLOAT SECONDS those are different numbers: 0.1 added eight times is
// 0.7999999999999999, which times 10 fps truncates to frame 7 where 8 is right.
// So this loop lands on 7 — one short of home — and an eight-frame walk cycle
// visibly hitches once per stride.  Summing integer microseconds cannot do that,
// which is the whole reason `advance` takes them.
//
// Hand-computed: after tick k the elapsed is exactly k x 100000 us, so the frame
// is k % 8 — 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 0.
fn test_ticking_a_whole_loop_lands_exactly_on_zero() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(0, 8, 10.0, stage::LOOP);   // 8 frames at 10 fps = 0.8 s
  n = walker(st, q);
  st.compose();
  want = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 0];
  for k in 0..8 {
    st.advance(100000);                            // 0.1 s, one tick at a time
    assert(st.frame_of(n) == (want[k] ?? -1),
           "tick {k + 1}: got {st.frame_of(n)}, want {want[k] ?? -1}");
  }
}

// ⚠ FRAME-RATE INDEPENDENT.  Stepping at 60 Hz and at 30 Hz must agree at the same
// elapsed time — the property that makes a recorded input stream replayable, and
// therefore the property co-op determinism rests on.
fn test_thirty_and_sixty_hertz_agree() {
  fast = stage::stage_new();
  qf = fast.add_sequence(0, 6, 12.0, stage::LOOP);
  nf = walker(fast, qf);
  fast.compose();
  slow = stage::stage_new();
  qs = slow.add_sequence(0, 6, 12.0, stage::LOOP);
  ns = walker(slow, qs);
  slow.compose();

  // ⚠ Two 60 Hz steps SUM EXACTLY to one 30 Hz step in integer microseconds —
  // 2 x 16667 = 33334 — which is the whole reason the delta is not a float.
  for i in 0..60 {
    fast.advance(16667);
    fast.advance(16667);
    slow.advance(33334);
    assert(fast.frame_of(nf) == slow.frame_of(ns),
           "step {i}: 60 Hz says {fast.frame_of(nf)}, 30 Hz says {slow.frame_of(ns)}");
  }
}

// ONCE stops on the last frame and stays there — it does not wrap, and it does not
// run off the end of the atlas.
fn test_once_stops_on_the_last_frame() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(10, 3, 10.0, stage::ONCE);   // cells 10, 11, 12
  n = walker(st, q);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 10, "starts at the sequence's first cell");
  st.advance(250000);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 12, "reaches the last");
  st.advance(50000000);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 12, "and stays there rather than wrapping");
}

// ⚠ PING-PONG DOES NOT REPEAT ITS END FRAMES.  For four frames the cycle is
// 0 1 2 3 2 1 — period SIX, not eight.  A period of 2n shows frame 3 twice and
// frame 0 twice, which reads as a limp at both ends and is the classic off-by-one.
fn test_pingpong_reverses_without_stuttering() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(0, 4, 10.0, stage::PINGPONG);
  n = walker(st, q);
  st.compose();
  want = [0, 1, 2, 3, 2, 1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 2, 1];
  for i in 0..12 {
    got = st.frame_of(n);
    assert(got == (want[i] ?? -1), "tick {i}: got {got}, want {want[i] ?? -1}");
    st.advance(100000);
  }
}

// A single-frame sequence is legal and still: no modulo by zero, no flicker.
fn test_a_one_frame_sequence_is_still() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(7, 1, 30.0, stage::PINGPONG);
  n = walker(st, q);
  st.compose();
  for _i in 0..10 {
    assert(st.frame_of(n) == 7, "a one-frame sequence never leaves its cell");
    st.advance(50000);
  }
}

// A still sprite ignores the clock entirely — the degenerate case proved.
fn test_a_still_sprite_never_animates() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0 });
  st.compose();
  st.advance(100000000);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 0, "no sequence, no motion");
}

// ⚠ THE FRAME REACHES THE INSTANCE BUFFER as ONE float, and the rest of the
// instance is untouched by a step — four floats per animating sprite is what this
// design exists to avoid.
fn test_a_step_dirties_exactly_one_float_per_sprite() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(0, 8, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  _ = walker(st, q);
  _ = walker(st, q);
  st.compose();
  before = st.pack_instances();
  st.advance(100000);
  after = st.pack_instances();
  changed = 0;
  for i in 0..len(before) { if before[i] != after[i] { changed = changed + 1; } }
  assert(changed == 2, "two sprites, one float each — got {changed}");
  assert(before[stage::OFF_CELL] != after[stage::OFF_CELL], "and it is the frame index");
}

// `restart` puts a sequence back to its beginning, which is what a state change
// (idle -> walk) needs.
fn test_restart_returns_to_the_first_frame() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(0, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  n = walker(st, q);
  st.compose();
  st.advance(250000);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) != 0, "somewhere in the middle");
  st.restart(n);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 0, "and back to the start");
}

tests/atmosphere.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 L3 — per-layer atmosphere: fog and blur.
//
// A layer already carries a parallax factor (P2).  Give it a fog colour, a
// density and a blur radius, and *distant, hazy, out-of-focus* becomes LAYER
// DATA rather than an effects pipeline.
use stage;
use graphics;

EPS = 0.000001;
fn near(a: float, b: float) -> boolean { a - b < EPS and b - a < EPS }
fn differing(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if (a.data[i] ?? 0) != (b.data[i] ?? 0) { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}
fn luminance(cv: const graphics::Canvas) -> integer {
  t = 0;
  for v in cv.data { t = t + ((v >> 16) & 255) + ((v >> 8) & 255) + (v & 255); }
  t
}
fn band(st: stage::Stage, x: float, w: float, layer: integer, colour: integer) -> integer {
  st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: x, pl_y: 0.0, pl_w: w, pl_h: 10.0,
                                 pl_layer: layer, pl_colour: colour })
}

// ── fog: the degenerate cases proved, as with P2's factor 1.0 ────────────────

// ⚠ DENSITY 0 IS BIT-IDENTICAL TO NO FOG.  Setting a fog colour must not tint
// anything until an author asks for density, or the feature changes games that
// never used it.
fn test_fog_at_density_zero_is_bit_identical() {
  plain = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(plain, 0.0, 20.0, 0, 0xc03030);
  plain.compose();
  ca = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  plain.render_view(0, ca);

  fogged = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(fogged, 0.0, 20.0, 0, 0xc03030);
  fogged.set_layer_fog(0, 0x2040ff, 0.0);
  fogged.compose();
  cb = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  fogged.render_view(0, cb);
  assert(differing(ca, cb) == 0, "{differing(ca, cb)} pixels moved at density 0");
}

// ⚠ AND DENSITY 1 IS EXACTLY THE FOG COLOUR — the other end pinned, so the lerp
// cannot be subtly wrong in the middle without one end giving it away.
fn test_fog_at_density_one_is_exactly_the_fog_colour() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(st, 0.0, 20.0, 0, 0xc03030);
  st.set_layer_fog(0, 0x2040ff, 1.0);
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(0, cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(5, 5) == 0xff2040ff,
         "full fog is the fog colour exactly — got {cv.get_pixel(5, 5)}");
}

// Hand-computed on the way between, with the same rounding the haze mix uses:
// t = round(0.5 * 255) = 128, and (192,48,48) toward (32,64,255) gives
// (112, 56, 152) = 0x703898.
fn test_fog_between_the_ends_is_hand_computed() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(st, 0.0, 20.0, 0, 0xc03030);
  st.set_layer_fog(0, 0x2040ff, 0.5);
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(0, cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(5, 5) == 0xff703898, "half fog — got {cv.get_pixel(5, 5)}");
}

// ⚠ FOG IS PER LAYER, so a foggy distance leaves the foreground alone.  That is
// the whole reason it is layer data and not a stage-wide setting.
fn test_fog_reaches_only_its_own_layer() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(st, 0.0, 10.0, 0, 0xc03030);     // background
  _ = band(st, 20.0, 10.0, 1, 0xc03030);    // foreground, same colour
  st.set_layer_fog(0, 0x2040ff, 1.0);
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(0, cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(5, 5) == 0xff2040ff, "the background is fogged out");
  assert(cv.get_pixel(25, 5) == 0xffc03030, "the foreground is untouched");
}

// ── blur ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// Radius 0 is no blur at all — the degenerate case, again bit-identical.
fn test_blur_radius_zero_is_bit_identical() {
  plain = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(plain, 0.0, 20.0, 0, 0xc03030);
  plain.compose();
  ca = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  plain.render_view(0, ca);

  z = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(z, 0.0, 20.0, 0, 0xc03030);
  z.set_layer_blur(0, 0);
  z.compose();
  cb = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  z.render_view(0, cb);
  assert(differing(ca, cb) == 0, "radius 0 moved {differing(ca, cb)} pixels");
}

// ⚠ A UNIFORM FIELD BLURS TO ITSELF, EXACTLY.  This is the sharpest possible
// statement of *the border does not darken*: every sample is equal, so every
// average is that value — unless the kernel reads outside the image and counts
// black, which is the classic bug and shows up as a dark frame around the edge.
fn test_a_uniform_layer_blurs_to_itself() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(st, 0.0, 40.0, 0, 0x808080);     // fills the whole canvas
  st.set_layer_blur(0, 2);
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(0, cv);
  off = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v != 0xff808080 { off = off + 1; } }
  assert(off == 0, "{off} pixels changed — the border darkened");
}

// ⚠ AND A NON-UNIFORM LAYER PRESERVES TOTAL LUMINANCE.  Sampling outside as
// black loses light everywhere the kernel overhangs; clamping to the edge keeps
// it.  Rounding may move the total by a pixel or two, never by a border's worth.
fn test_blur_preserves_total_luminance() {
  sharp = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(sharp, 0.0, 12.0, 0, 0xffffff);
  _ = band(sharp, 26.0, 14.0, 0, 0x404040);
  sharp.compose();
  ca = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  sharp.render_view(0, ca);

  soft = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(soft, 0.0, 12.0, 0, 0xffffff);
  _ = band(soft, 26.0, 14.0, 0, 0x404040);
  soft.set_layer_blur(0, 2);
  soft.compose();
  cb = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  soft.render_view(0, cb);

  assert(differing(ca, cb) > 0, "the blur did something, or this proves nothing");
  before = luminance(ca);
  after = luminance(cb);
  d = if after > before { after - before } else { before - after };
  assert(d * 200 < before,
         "luminance moved {before} -> {after} (delta {d}); a lost border shows here");
}

// ⚠ BLUR IS PER LAYER, BEFORE THE LAYERS ABOVE COMPOSITE — which a fullscreen
// blur cannot do and is the entire point.  A blurred distance under a sharp
// foreground: the far edge goes soft, the near one stays a hard step.
fn test_a_sharp_foreground_over_a_blurred_background_stays_sharp() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = band(st, 0.0, 20.0, 0, 0xffffff);     // background, edge at x = 20
  st.set_layer_blur(0, 2);
  _ = band(st, 30.0, 6.0, 1, 0x00ff00);     // foreground, edge at x = 30
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(40, 10, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(0, cv);

  // The background edge is SOFT: the pixel on the boundary is neither colour.
  e = (cv.get_pixel(20, 5) >> 16) & 255;
  assert(e > 0 and e < 255, "the background edge is a gradient, got {e}");
  // The foreground edge is HARD: background one side, full colour the other.
  assert(cv.get_pixel(29, 5) == 0xff000000, "nothing just left of the foreground");
  assert(cv.get_pixel(30, 5) == 0xff00ff00,
         "and full colour immediately right — a fullscreen blur would soften this");
}

// ── layer data lives in one place ───────────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ PARALLAX, FOG AND BLUR ARE ONE LAYER'S FACTS, in one record.  They were
// three parallel vectors in an earlier draft, which is exactly the shape that
// put a sequence's name on another sequence's cells in P5: each list stays
// well-formed on its own and only the correspondence goes wrong.
fn test_a_layers_facts_travel_together() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_layer_blur(3, 4);                  // reaches PAST the layers below it
  st.set_parallax(1, 0.25);
  st.set_layer_fog(3, 0x102030, 0.75);
  assert(near(st.parallax_of(1), 0.25), "layer 1 kept its parallax");
  assert(near(st.parallax_of(3), 1.0), "layer 3 was never given one");
  assert(st.layer_blur(3) == 4, "layer 3 kept its blur");
  assert(st.layer_blur(1) == 0, "layer 1 has none");
  assert(st.layer_fog_colour(3) == 0x102030 and near(st.layer_fog_density(3), 0.75),
         "and its fog");
  assert(near(st.layer_fog_density(1), 0.0), "which layer 1 does not have");
}

// A layer nobody configured is the plain one: parallax 1, no fog, no blur.
fn test_an_unconfigured_layer_is_plain() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  assert(near(st.parallax_of(7), 1.0), "the world plane");
  assert(st.layer_blur(7) == 0, "sharp");
  assert(near(st.layer_fog_density(7), 0.0), "and clear");
}

// ⚠ `blur_region` IS PUBLIC, so its own radius guard is its contract rather than
// a second copy of the layer's policy — `blur_layer` decides whether a layer
// blurs at all, and returns before this is reached.  Two guards, two jobs; each
// is gated where it lives, which is what keeps them from becoming one rule with
// two homes.
fn test_blur_region_of_radius_zero_is_the_identity() {
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 4, 0xff000000);
  cv.set_pixel(1, 1, 0xffffffff);
  cv.set_pixel(6, 2, 0xff204060);
  before: vector<integer> = [];
  for v in cv.data { before += [v]; }
  stage::blur_region(cv, 0, 0, 8, 4, 0);
  moved = 0;
  for i in 0..len(cv.data) { if (cv.data[i] ?? 0) != (before[i] ?? 0) { moved = moved + 1; } }
  assert(moved == 0, "radius 0 changed {moved} pixels");
  // And a real radius does move it, or the cell above is vacuous.
  stage::blur_region(cv, 0, 0, 8, 4, 1);
  spread = 0;
  for i in 0..len(cv.data) { if (cv.data[i] ?? 0) != (before[i] ?? 0) { spread = spread + 1; } }
  assert(spread > 0, "radius 1 must actually blur");
}

tests/batching.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 A3a — the batcher: contiguous runs, and the instance buffer.
//
// The design risk here is not speed, it is ORDER and STRIDE.  A batcher that
// gathers every command of one atlas together is faster and draws the wrong
// picture; a stride or offset off by one fails as garbage geometry rather than as
// an error.  So every expected value below is hand-computed, and the interleave
// case asserts the COST rather than hiding it.
use stage;

EPS = 0.0001;
fn near(a: single, b: float) -> boolean {
  d = (a as float) - b;
  d < EPS and -d < EPS
}

fn sized(atlas: integer, x: float, colour: integer) -> stage::Place {
  stage::Place { pl_x: x, pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: atlas, pl_colour: colour }
}

// One atlas: everything merges into a single run.
fn test_one_atlas_is_one_run() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  for i in 0..5 { _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(7, (i * 10) as float, 0x102030)); }
  st.compose();
  bs = st.batches();
  assert(len(bs) == 1, "one atlas gives one run, got {len(bs)}");
  assert(bs[0].b_atlas == 7 and bs[0].b_first == 0 and bs[0].b_count == 5,
         "the run covers every instance");
}

// Grouped by atlas in scene order: two runs, and the boundary is exactly where
// the atlas changes.
fn test_runs_break_where_the_atlas_changes() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(1, 0.0, 0x111111));
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(1, 10.0, 0x222222));
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(2, 20.0, 0x333333));
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(2, 30.0, 0x444444));
  st.compose();
  bs = st.batches();
  assert(len(bs) == 2, "expected 2 runs, got {len(bs)}");
  assert(bs[0].b_atlas == 1 and bs[0].b_first == 0 and bs[0].b_count == 2, "first run");
  assert(bs[1].b_atlas == 2 and bs[1].b_first == 2 and bs[1].b_count == 2, "second run");
}

// ⚠ THE COST, ASSERTED RATHER THAN HIDDEN.  Interleaving two atlases
// sprite-by-sprite gives ONE INSTANCE PER RUN — four draws for four sprites.
// A batcher that answered 2 here would be sorting, and sorting reorders the
// picture under alpha blending.  The cure is packing, not batching: sprites that
// draw near each other must share a page.
fn test_interleaving_two_atlases_costs_a_run_each() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(1, 0.0, 0x111111));
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(2, 10.0, 0x222222));
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(1, 20.0, 0x333333));
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(2, 30.0, 0x444444));
  st.compose();
  bs = st.batches();
  assert(len(bs) == 4, "interleaved atlases must NOT be gathered; got {len(bs)} runs");
  for i in 0..4 { assert(bs[i].b_count == 1 and bs[i].b_first == i, "run {i} is one instance"); }
}

// A batch indexes the packed buffer directly, so the two walks must agree about
// which nodes are skipped.
fn test_batches_and_packing_agree_about_what_is_skipped() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(1, 0.0, 0x111111));
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 5.0 });                       // no size
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_visible: false });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, sized(1, 20.0, 0x222222));
  st.compose();
  bs = st.batches();
  floats = st.pack_instances();
  assert(len(bs) == 1 and bs[0].b_count == 2, "only the two drawable nodes count");
  assert(len(floats) == 2 * stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE,
         "the buffer holds exactly the batched instances: {len(floats)}");
}

// THE STRIDE GATE.  A node at (10, 20), unit scale, no rotation, size 4x4,
// colour 0xff8040, alpha 1: the affine columns carry the SIZE, so
// a = 1*4, b = 0, c = 0, d = 1*4, and the translation is the origin (10, 20).
// rgba is 255/255, 128/255, 64/255, 1.
fn test_instance_layout_is_exact() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_y: 20.0, pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0xff8040 });
  st.compose();
  f = st.pack_instances();
  assert(len(f) == stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE, "one instance is one stride");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 0], 4.0), "a = sx * w");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 1], 0.0), "b");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 2], 0.0), "c");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 3], 4.0), "d = sy * h");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 4], 10.0), "tx is the origin x");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 5], 20.0), "ty is the origin y");
  // ⚠ This slot carries a FRAME INDEX and the atlas grid, not a uv rect — an
  // animating sprite must dirty one float per frame, never four.
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_CELL + 0], 0.0), "a still sprite is on frame 0");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_CELL + 1], 1.0) and near(f[stage::OFF_CELL + 2], 1.0),
         "and a 1x1 grid by default");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_RGBA + 0], 1.0), "r");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_RGBA + 1], 128.0 / 255.0), "g");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_RGBA + 2], 64.0 / 255.0), "b");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_RGBA + 3], 1.0), "alpha");
}

// The second instance must start exactly one stride in — the off-by-one that
// fails as garbage geometry rather than as an error.
fn test_the_second_instance_starts_one_stride_in() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 1.0, pl_y: 2.0, pl_w: 1.0, pl_h: 1.0 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 7.0, pl_y: 9.0, pl_w: 1.0, pl_h: 1.0 });
  st.compose();
  f = st.pack_instances();
  assert(len(f) == 2 * stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE, "two instances");
  assert(near(f[stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE + 4], 7.0), "second tx");
  assert(near(f[stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE + 5], 9.0), "second ty");
}

// Rotation and scale reach the affine, so the shader keeps drawing a unit quad.
// A quarter turn with size 4x4: local +x becomes world +y, so a = 0, b = 4,
// and local +y becomes world -x, so c = -4, d = 0.
fn test_rotation_reaches_the_instance_affine() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_rot: 1.5707963267948966, pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0 });
  st.compose();
  f = st.pack_instances();
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 0], 0.0), "a");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 1], 4.0), "b");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 2], -4.0), "c");
  assert(near(f[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 3], 0.0), "d");
}

tests/camera.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 P2 — the camera, and the two scroll modes as ONE mechanism.
//
// Riding left and right across a world is a camera pan; a background that lags is
// the same pan at a smaller factor.  Flat scrolling is every factor at 1.0, and
// that is proved here as a degenerate case rather than maintained as a second path.
use stage;
use graphics;

fn shot(st: const stage::Stage) -> graphics::Canvas {
  cv = graphics::canvas(64, 32, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  cv
}
fn same(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> boolean {
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if a.data[i] != b.data[i] { return false; } }
  true
}

// FLAT MODE IS THE DEGENERATE CASE: with every factor 1.0, panning the camera is
// pixel-identical to having placed every node that much further left.
fn test_a_pan_equals_moving_every_node() {
  panned = stage::stage_new();
  _ = panned.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 30.0, pl_y: 10.0, pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0,
                                         pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  _ = panned.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 45.0, pl_y: 4.0, pl_w: 6.0, pl_h: 12.0,
                                         pl_colour: 0x0000ff });
  panned.compose();
  panned.set_camera(12.0, 3.0);

  byhand = stage::stage_new();
  _ = byhand.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 18.0, pl_y: 7.0, pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0,
                                         pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  _ = byhand.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 33.0, pl_y: 1.0, pl_w: 6.0, pl_h: 12.0,
                                         pl_colour: 0x0000ff });
  byhand.compose();

  assert(same(shot(panned), shot(byhand)),
         "a pan of (12,3) draws exactly what placing the nodes 12 left and 3 up draws");
}

// A layer feels its OWN share of the pan: half the factor, half the movement.
fn test_a_layer_moves_by_its_factor() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_layer: 0 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_layer: 1 });
  st.set_parallax(0, 0.5);
  st.set_parallax(1, 1.0);
  st.compose();
  st.set_camera(20.0, 8.0);
  back = st.cam_offset(0);
  front = st.cam_offset(1);
  assert(back.p_x == -10.0 and back.p_y == -4.0, "half factor, half the pan");
  assert(front.p_x == -20.0 and front.p_y == -8.0, "full factor, all of it");
}

// A layer with no factor set behaves as 1.0 — the world plane, without ceremony.
fn test_an_unset_layer_is_the_world_plane() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_camera(7.0, 0.0);
  assert(st.parallax_of(3) == 1.0, "unset layers are 1.0");
  assert(st.cam_offset(3).p_x == -7.0, "and move with the world");
}

// ⚠ THE PROPERTY THE DESIGN TURNS ON: a pure camera move re-uploads NOTHING.
// Baking the camera into node positions would rewrite every node and re-upload the
// whole instance buffer on every scrolled frame.
fn test_a_camera_move_does_not_dirty_the_buffer() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  for i in 0..8 {
    _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: (i * 4) as float, pl_w: 3.0, pl_h: 3.0 });
  }
  st.compose();
  before = st.pack_instances();
  for i in 0..100 { st.set_camera((i * 3) as float, 0.0); }
  after = st.pack_instances();
  assert(len(before) == len(after), "the buffer is the same size");
  differs = false;
  for i in 0..len(before) { if before[i] != after[i] { differs = true; } }
  assert(!differs, "and 100 camera moves changed not one float of it");
}

// ⚠ PICKING UN-APPLIES THE CAMERA PER LAYER.  One screen point is a different
// world point in each layer, so a single inverse picks the wrong thing the moment
// a layer lags.
fn test_picking_un_applies_the_camera_per_layer() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  back = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 0.0, pl_y: 0.0, pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0,
                                        pl_layer: 0 });
  front = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 40.0, pl_y: 0.0, pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0,
                                         pl_layer: 1 });
  st.set_parallax(0, 0.5);
  st.compose();
  st.set_camera(20.0, 0.0);
  // back  is drawn at 0  - 20*0.5 = -10 .. 0   -> screen x 5 misses it
  // front is drawn at 40 - 20*1.0 =  20 .. 30  -> screen x 25 hits it
  assert(st.pick(25.0, 5.0) == front, "the front layer is where its own factor puts it");
  assert(st.pick(-5.0, 5.0) == back, "and the lagging layer where ITS factor puts it");
  assert(st.pick(5.0, 5.0) == -1, "between them, nothing");
}

// Parallax TRANSLATES; it never resizes.  Distance-as-smaller is the depth cue's
// job, which scales about the origin and keeps a sprite's feet planted.
fn test_parallax_does_not_resize() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_layer: 0 });
  st.set_parallax(0, 0.25);
  st.compose();
  st.set_camera(100.0, 0.0);
  b = st.bounds_of(n);
  assert(b.b_x1 - b.b_x0 > 9.99 and b.b_x1 - b.b_x0 < 10.01,
         "the node is still 10 wide at any parallax");
}

tests/clipping.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 A6 — clipping: inherited, intersected, and breaking a batch.
//
// Every count below is hand-computed from the rectangles, and the classification
// checks the `other` bucket is 0 — a pixel of a third colour would mean the clip
// half-applied, which a total count alone would hide.
use stage;
use graphics;

fn count_of(cv: const graphics::Canvas, want: integer) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v == want { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}

// A clipping parent cuts its child at the exact boundary.
// Parent clips to (0,0)-(10,10); the child covers (5,5)-(25,25); the visible part
// is (5,5)-(10,10) = 5 x 5 = 25 pixels.
fn test_a_child_is_cut_at_the_parent_boundary() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  p = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_clips: true });
  _ = st.node_add(p, stage::Place { pl_x: 5.0, pl_y: 5.0, pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 20.0,
                                    pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(32, 32, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff00ff00) == 25,
         "5x5 survives the clip, got {count_of(cv, 0xff00ff00)}");
}

// Nested two deep: the inner clip is the INTERSECTION, never the innermost alone.
// Outer clips to (0,0)-(20,20); inner clips to (10,10)-(40,40); the intersection is
// (10,10)-(20,20).  A child covering everything therefore shows 10 x 10 = 100.
fn test_nested_clips_intersect() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  outer = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_clips: true });
  inner = st.node_add(outer, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_y: 10.0, pl_w: 30.0,
                                            pl_h: 30.0, pl_clips: true });
  _ = st.node_add(inner, stage::Place { pl_x: -10.0, pl_y: -10.0, pl_w: 60.0,
                                        pl_h: 60.0, pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(48, 48, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff00ff00) == 100,
         "the intersection is 10x10, got {count_of(cv, 0xff00ff00)}");
}

// Clips that do not overlap show nothing at all — the empty intersection.
fn test_disjoint_clips_show_nothing() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  outer = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_clips: true });
  inner = st.node_add(outer, stage::Place { pl_x: 20.0, pl_y: 20.0, pl_w: 10.0,
                                            pl_h: 10.0, pl_clips: true });
  _ = st.node_add(inner, stage::Place { pl_w: 40.0, pl_h: 40.0, pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(48, 48, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff00ff00) == 0, "disjoint clips leave nothing visible");
}

// A clipper does not clip its SIBLINGS, only its own subtree.
fn test_a_clip_reaches_only_its_own_subtree() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  clipper = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0, pl_clips: true });
  _ = st.node_add(clipper, stage::Place { pl_w: 40.0, pl_h: 40.0, pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 20.0, pl_y: 0.0, pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0x0000ff });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(48, 48, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff00ff00) == 64, "the clipped child is 8x8");
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff0000ff) == 100, "the sibling is untouched at 10x10");
}

// The degenerate case, proved rather than assumed: no clip anywhere is the same
// picture as before clipping existed.
fn test_an_unclipped_scene_is_unchanged() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 3.0, pl_y: 4.0, pl_w: 6.0, pl_h: 5.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(16, 16, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff00ff00) == 30, "6x5 = 30 pixels, all of them");
}

// ⚠ A CLIP CHANGE BREAKS A BATCH.  Two nodes on the SAME atlas under DIFFERENT
