// clips are two runs — a scissor is state, exactly like a texture. Grouping by
// atlas alone would draw the second under the first's scissor.
fn test_a_clip_change_breaks_a_run() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  // ⚠ A clip region is SIZED so it has a rect, and INVISIBLE so it is not itself
  // drawn — the idiom for scaffolding that shapes its subtree without painting.
  a = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_clips: true,
                                     pl_visible: false });
  _ = st.node_add(a, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 1 });
  b = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 20.0, pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_clips: true,
                                     pl_visible: false });
  _ = st.node_add(b, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 1 });
  st.compose();
  bs = st.batches();
  // Only the two children reach the list — same atlas, different scissor.
  assert(len(bs) == 2, "one atlas but two clips is two runs, got {len(bs)}");
  assert(bs[0].b_clipped and bs[1].b_clipped, "both runs carry a scissor");
  assert(bs[0].b_cx0 != bs[1].b_cx0, "and the scissors differ");
}

// Two nodes under the SAME clip stay one run — the break is a change, not a clip.
fn test_one_clip_is_still_one_run() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  c = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_clips: true,
                                     pl_visible: false });
  _ = st.node_add(c, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 1 });
  _ = st.node_add(c, stage::Place { pl_x: 6.0, pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 1 });
  st.compose();
  assert(len(st.batches()) == 1, "same atlas, same clip, one run");
}

// ⚠ A CLIP AND A CAMERA, TOGETHER — the off-diagonal cell neither phase tested.
// The clip is derived in WORLD space by `compose`; what it cuts has already been
// moved by the camera.  Applying it raw cut the wrong region, and a pan wider
// than the node cut ALL of it: a clipped panel silently disappeared the moment
// the camera moved.  A6 and P2 were each green alone.
// A clipping parent at world (0,0) 10x10 with a child filling it.  Pan the
// camera by 20: the whole thing should move 20 left and stay 10x10.
fn test_a_clip_follows_its_content_under_a_camera() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  p = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_x: 30.0, pl_y: 0.0,
                                     pl_clips: true });
  _ = st.node_add(p, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_colour: 0xc03030 });
  st.compose();
  a = graphics::canvas(60, 20, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(a);
  assert(count_of(a, 0xffc03030) == 100, "unpanned: 10x10 = 100 px, got {count_of(a, 0xffc03030)}");
  assert(a.get_pixel(35, 5) == 0xffc03030, "and it is at x=30..40");

  st.set_camera(20.0, 0.0);
  b = graphics::canvas(60, 20, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(b);
  assert(count_of(b, 0xffc03030) == 100,
         "panned: STILL 10x10 = 100 px, got {count_of(b, 0xffc03030)}");
  assert(b.get_pixel(15, 5) == 0xffc03030, "moved to x=10..20");
}

tests/compositing.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 A5 — alpha and tint, composited, with every expected byte worked out by
// hand.  Premultiplied throughout: `over` is `src + dst*(1-a)` with `src` already
// multiplied, because straight alpha darkens an anti-aliased edge under linear
// filtering and this stack is made of overlapping soft-edged sprites.
use stage;
use graphics;

// Classify a canvas by EXACT colour, dryopea's RENDERER.md R0 technique: a
// byte-diff says *different*, a classification says *what*.  `other` must read 0.
fn count_of(cv: const graphics::Canvas, want: integer) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v == want { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}
fn other_than(cv: const graphics::Canvas, a: integer, b: integer) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v != a and v != b { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}

// Alpha 1 is exactly the colour — the degenerate case proved, not assumed.
fn test_full_alpha_is_the_colour() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xc03030 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(1, 1) == 0xffc03030, "opaque paints the colour exactly");
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xffc03030) == 16, "a 4x4 rect is 16 pixels");
  assert(other_than(cv, 0xffc03030, 0xff000000) == 0, "no third colour anywhere");
}

// Alpha 0 leaves the destination untouched.
fn test_zero_alpha_paints_nothing() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xffffff,
                                     pl_alpha: 0.0 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff102030);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff102030) == 64, "every pixel is still the background");
}

// THE HAND-COMPUTED CELL.  alpha 0.5 -> ia = round(0.5*255) = 128.
//   premultiplied source red = (255*128 + 127) / 255 = 32767/255 = 128
//   over(128, 0, 128)        = (128*255 + 0*127 + 127) / 255 = 32767/255 = 128
// so pure red at half alpha over black is exactly 0xff800000.
fn test_half_alpha_over_black() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xff0000,
                                     pl_alpha: 0.5 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(1, 1) == 0xff800000,
         "half red over black is 0x80, got {cv.get_pixel(1, 1)}");
  assert(other_than(cv, 0xff800000, 0xff000000) == 0, "no third colour");
}

// Half alpha over a NON-black destination — the case that catches a composite
// which ignores what is underneath.
//   dst = 0x40, src premultiplied = 128
//   over(128, 64, 128) = (128*255 + 64*127 + 127) / 255 = (32640+8128+127)/255
//                      = 40895/255 = 160
fn test_half_alpha_over_a_colour() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xff0000,
                                     pl_alpha: 0.5 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff404040);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  got = cv.get_pixel(1, 1);
  assert(got == 0xffa02020, "half red over 0x40 grey is 0xa02020, got {got}");
}

// Two translucent nodes stack IN ORDER, and the second sees the first's result.
//
// ⚠ Work the channels SEPARATELY — the mistake this comment replaces was carrying
// red's destination into green and blue.  After node 1 the pixel is (128, 0, 0),
// not (128, 128, 128).  Node 2 is white at 0.5, so its premultiplied source is
// (128, 128, 128) and:
//   R: over(128, 128, 128) = (128*255 + 128*127 + 127)/255 = 49023/255 = 192
//   G: over(128,   0, 128) = (128*255 +   0*127 + 127)/255 = 32767/255 = 128
//   B: same as G                                                        = 128
fn test_two_translucent_nodes_stack_in_order() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xff0000,
                                     pl_alpha: 0.5 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xffffff,
                                     pl_alpha: 0.5 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  got = cv.get_pixel(1, 1);
  assert(got == 0xffc08080, "red then white, both half — expect 0xc08080, got {got}");
}

// Tint is the node's own colour: two nodes of the same shape and alpha differ only
// by it, and the difference survives compositing.
fn test_tint_reaches_the_composite() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 0.0, pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 2.0, pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 4.0, pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 2.0, pl_colour: 0x0000ff });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(1, 1) == 0xff00ff00 and cv.get_pixel(5, 1) == 0xff0000ff,
         "each node keeps its own tint");
}

// The two paths agree where alpha is 1 — `render` (a lavition_ui command list) and
// `render_stage` must not drift into two different pictures.
fn test_the_two_render_paths_agree_at_full_alpha() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 2.0, pl_y: 3.0, pl_w: 5.0, pl_h: 4.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0x3060a0 });
  st.compose();
  a = graphics::canvas(16, 16, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(a);
  b = graphics::canvas(16, 16, 0xff000000);
  stage::render(st.draw_list(), b);
  same = true;
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if a.data[i] != b.data[i] { same = false; } }
  assert(same, "render_stage and render draw the same opaque picture");
}

tests/depth.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 P1 — layer and depth: how a 2-D scene presents a 3-D world.
//
// The case these exist for: buildings authored in 3-D and projected to a
// footprint, with mobs, workers and a player as sprites that must sort correctly
// against them.  A sprite standing in front of a wall covers it; one standing
// behind it does not; and neither may flicker between frames.
use stage;
use graphics;

fn at(depth: float, layer: integer, colour: integer) -> stage::Place {
  stage::Place { pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0, pl_depth: depth, pl_layer: layer,
                 pl_colour: colour }
}

fn seq(st: const stage::Stage) -> text {
  out = "";
  for i in st.order() { out += "{i},"; }
  out
}

// Depth decides, whatever the insertion order was.
fn test_depth_orders_the_draw() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, at(10.0, 0, 0x111111));   // 0 — far (large depth)
  _ = st.node_add(-1, at(-5.0, 0, 0x222222));   // 1 — near
  _ = st.node_add(-1, at(0.0, 0, 0x333333));    // 2 — middle
  st.compose();
  // Depth is distance INTO the screen, so the far one draws first.
  assert(seq(st) == "0,2,1,", "far to near, got {seq(st)}");
}

// ⚠ LAYER OUTRANKS DEPTH ENTIRELY.  A UI element at a huge depth still draws over
// a world sprite at a tiny one — that is what makes background / world / UI bands
// rather than a single number every node has to get right.
fn test_layer_outranks_depth() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  // ⚠ The depths are chosen so layer-first and depth-first DISAGREE.  With the UI
  // element the FURTHER of the two, a sort that consulted depth first would draw it
  // underneath — and an earlier version of this test used depths that gave the same
  // answer either way, so it could not tell them apart.
  _ = st.node_add(-1, at(1000.0, 1, 0x111111));   // 0 — UI band, very "far"
  _ = st.node_add(-1, at(-1000.0, 0, 0x222222));  // 1 — world band, very "near"
  st.compose();
  assert(seq(st) == "1,0,", "the world band draws first however near it is");
}

// ⚠ THE SORT IS STABLE, and that is the third key's whole job.  Two sprites on the
// same footprint — a worker standing where another stands — must not swap from
// frame to frame.
fn test_equal_depth_keeps_insertion_order() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  // Six equal depths, deliberately more than the merge width so the tie-break is
  // exercised at every level of the merge rather than only in one pass.
  for _i in 0..6 { _ = st.node_add(-1, at(3.0, 0, 0x111111)); }
  st.compose();
  assert(seq(st) == "0,1,2,3,4,5,", "ties keep insertion order, got {seq(st)}");
  st.compose();
  assert(seq(st) == "0,1,2,3,4,5,", "and composing again does not reshuffle them");
}

// The 2.5-D idiom, end to end: `depth = -y` makes a sprite lower on the screen
// draw in front.  A worker at y=40 covers a wall at y=20.
fn test_depth_from_minus_y_puts_the_lower_sprite_in_front() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  wall = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 0.0, pl_y: 20.0, pl_w: 16.0, pl_h: 16.0,
                                        pl_depth: -20.0, pl_colour: 0x808080 });
  worker = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 4.0, pl_y: 28.0, pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0,
                                          pl_depth: -28.0, pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  st.compose();
  assert(st.order()[1] == worker, "the lower sprite draws last");
  cv = graphics::canvas(32, 48, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(6, 30) == 0xff00ff00, "and it covers the wall where they overlap");
  assert(cv.get_pixel(6, 22) == 0xff808080, "the wall still shows above it");
  assert(wall == 0, "insertion order is unchanged by sorting");
}

// Picking follows the same order, so what you click is what you see on top.
fn test_picking_follows_the_draw_order() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  // ⚠ The NEAR node is added FIRST, so the drawn order (far, near) is the reverse
  // of the index order.  A pick that walked indices instead of the order would
  // answer `far` here and would have agreed with this test when the two coincided.
  near = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_depth: -5.0 });
  far = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_depth: 5.0 });
  st.compose();
  assert(near == 0 and far == 1, "indices are insertion order");
  assert(seq(st) == "1,0,", "but the drawn order is far then near");
  assert(st.pick(10.0, 10.0) == near, "and the pick follows the DRAWN order");
}

// A run still breaks on a state change, now measured along the SORTED order — the
// batcher sees the scene in the order it is drawn, never the order it was built.
fn test_batches_follow_the_sorted_order() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 1, pl_depth: 2.0 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 2, pl_depth: 1.0 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 1, pl_depth: 0.0 });
  st.compose();
  bs = st.batches();
  // sorted: atlas 1 (depth 0), atlas 2 (depth 1), atlas 1 (depth 2) -> three runs
  assert(len(bs) == 3, "three runs along the drawn order, got {len(bs)}");
  assert(bs[0].b_atlas == 1 and bs[1].b_atlas == 2 and bs[2].b_atlas == 1,
         "and in the drawn sequence");
}

// ── the 2.5-D depth cue ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// Off by default, and off means an IDENTICAL picture — the degenerate case proved
// rather than assumed, as with the camera's factor 1.0 and fog's density 0.
fn test_the_cue_is_off_by_default() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 4.0, pl_y: 4.0, pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0,
                                     pl_depth: 100.0, pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(32, 32, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  n = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v == 0xff00ff00 { n = n + 1; } }
  assert(n == 64, "8x8 undisturbed, got {n}");
  assert(st.haze_of(0) == 0.0, "and no haze");
}

// ⚠ THE PROPERTY THAT MATTERS: a distant node is drawn SMALLER but its ORIGIN
// DOES NOT MOVE.  A cue that shrank about the centre would slide a mob off the
// tile it is standing on, which is precisely the bug 2.5-D art shows worst.
fn test_distance_shrinks_a_sprite_without_moving_its_feet() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  // origin at the sprite's feet: bottom-centre of a 10x10 body.
  far = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 20.0, pl_y: 20.0, pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0,
                                       pl_ox: 5.0, pl_oy: 10.0, pl_depth: 100.0 });
  st.depth_cue(0.0, 100.0, 0.5, 0.0, 0x000000);
  st.compose();
  o = st.world_origin(far);
  assert(o.p_x > 19.99 and o.p_x < 20.01 and o.p_y > 19.99 and o.p_y < 20.01,
         "the feet stay at (20,20), got ({o.p_x}, {o.p_y})");
  b = st.bounds_of(far);
  assert(b.b_x1 - b.b_x0 > 4.99 and b.b_x1 - b.b_x0 < 5.01,
         "and the body is half as wide: {b.b_x1 - b.b_x0}");
  assert(b.b_y1 > 19.99 and b.b_y1 < 20.01, "its base still rests on the same line");
}

// The near plane is untouched, so only DISTANCE costs size.
fn test_the_near_plane_is_untouched() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  near = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_depth: 0.0 });
  st.depth_cue(0.0, 100.0, 0.25, 1.0, 0x000000);
  st.compose();
  b = st.bounds_of(near);
  assert(b.b_x1 - b.b_x0 > 9.99, "full size at the near plane");
  assert(st.haze_of(near) == 0.0, "and no haze");
}

// Haze interpolates, and is CLAMPED outside the planes so a stray depth cannot
// invert the effect.
fn test_haze_interpolates_and_clamps() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 2.0, pl_depth: 50.0 });
  b = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 2.0, pl_depth: 500.0 });
  c = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 2.0, pl_depth: -500.0 });
  st.depth_cue(0.0, 100.0, 1.0, 0.8, 0x000000);
  st.compose();
  h = st.haze_of(a);
  assert(h > 0.39 and h < 0.41, "half way is 0.4 of 0.8, got {h}");
  assert(st.haze_of(b) > 0.79 and st.haze_of(b) < 0.81, "beyond the far plane clamps");
  assert(st.haze_of(c) == 0.0, "nearer than the near plane clamps");
}

// A hazed node is painted toward the haze colour.  Fully hazed toward white, a
// pure red node paints white.
fn test_haze_fades_the_paint() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xff0000,
                                     pl_depth: 100.0 });
  st.depth_cue(0.0, 100.0, 1.0, 1.0, 0xffffff);
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(1, 1) == 0xffffffff,
         "fully hazed toward white, got {cv.get_pixel(1, 1)}");
}

tests/draw_list.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 A2 — the stage draws what a hand-written immediate-mode pass draws.
//
// This is a PARALLEL RUN, which is the point: the old way and the new way both
// exist and are compared exactly, pixel for pixel, rather than one being swapped
// in and eyeballed.  A software `Canvas` is the comparison surface because it is
// headless and deterministic — no window, no driver, no timing.
use stage;
use graphics;

fn canvas_equal(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> boolean {
  if a.width != b.width or a.height != b.height { return false; }
  for i in 0..len(a.data) {
    if a.data[i] != b.data[i] { return false; }
  }
  true
}

fn differing(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if a.data[i] != b.data[i] { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}

// THE GATE: a scene rendered through the stage is byte-identical to the same
// rectangles drawn by hand.
fn test_stage_matches_a_hand_written_pass() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_y: 12.0, pl_w: 30.0, pl_h: 20.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0xc03030 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 50.0, pl_y: 40.0, pl_w: 12.0, pl_h: 44.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0x2080c0 });
  st.compose();

  by_stage = graphics::canvas(128, 96, 0xff000000);
  stage::render(st.draw_list(), by_stage);

  by_hand = graphics::canvas(128, 96, 0xff000000);
  by_hand.fill_rect(10, 12, 30, 20, 0xffc03030);
  by_hand.fill_rect(50, 40, 12, 44, 0xff2080c0);

  assert(canvas_equal(by_stage, by_hand),
         "stage output differs from the hand-written pass in {differing(by_stage, by_hand)} pixels");
}

// A CHILD inherits its parent's transform, and the hand-written equivalent has to
// do that arithmetic itself — which is the ergonomic difference the arc exists for.
fn test_a_child_is_placed_through_its_parent() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  p = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 20.0, pl_y: 30.0, pl_sx: 2.0, pl_sy: 2.0 });
  _ = st.node_add(p, stage::Place { pl_x: 5.0, pl_y: 5.0, pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0,
                                    pl_colour: 0x40a040 });
  st.compose();

  by_stage = graphics::canvas(96, 96, 0xff000000);
  stage::render(st.draw_list(), by_stage);

  // by hand: the child sits at parent (20,30) + 2 x (5,5) = (30,40), sized 2 x 10.
  by_hand = graphics::canvas(96, 96, 0xff000000);
  by_hand.fill_rect(30, 40, 20, 20, 0xff40a040);

  assert(canvas_equal(by_stage, by_hand),
         "child placement differs in {differing(by_stage, by_hand)} pixels");
}

// The alpha seam.  A `DrawRect` carries 0xRRGGBB and `Canvas` reads 0xAARRGGBB,
// where a missing top byte means FULLY TRANSPARENT — graphics' own trap.
// Without `opaque` at the boundary the stage paints nothing at all, and the canvas
// stays exactly the colour it was cleared to.
//
// Example: @GFX-001
fn test_the_alpha_byte_is_added_at_the_boundary() {
  assert(stage::opaque(0xc03030) == 0xffc03030, "0xRRGGBB gains an opaque alpha");
  assert(stage::opaque(0x80112233) == 0x80112233, "an explicit alpha is left alone");

  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 0.0, pl_y: 0.0, pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0xc03030 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(8, 8, 0xff000000);
  stage::render(st.draw_list(), cv);
  assert(cv.get_pixel(1, 1) == 0xffc03030, "a 0xRRGGBB node paints, rather than vanishing");
}

// Scaffolding emits nothing: a parent that exists only to hold a transform has no
// size, and a zero-sized node must not reach the list at all.
fn test_a_sizeless_node_emits_nothing() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  p = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_y: 10.0 });
  _ = st.node_add(p, stage::Place { pl_w: 5.0, pl_h: 5.0, pl_colour: 0x111111 });
  hidden = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 5.0, pl_h: 5.0, pl_visible: false });
  st.compose();
  assert(len(st.draw_list()) == 1, "only the sized, visible node emits");
  assert(hidden == 2, "an invisible node still occupies its index");
}

// A list shaped exactly as `lavition_ui`'s `panel_draw_list` produces renders
// UNMODIFIED — no shim, no field rename.  The field names here are copied from
// that package; if this ever needs adapting, the command shape has drifted.
fn test_a_panel_shaped_list_renders_unmodified() {
  panel: vector<stage::DrawRect> = [
    stage::DrawRect { dr_rect: stage::UiRect { r_x: 2, r_y: 2, r_w: 40, r_h: 12 },
                      dr_colour: 0x303038 },
    stage::DrawRect { dr_rect: stage::UiRect { r_x: 4, r_y: 4, r_w: 12, r_h: 8 },
                      dr_colour: 0x5060a0 },
  ];
  by_list = graphics::canvas(64, 32, 0xff000000);
  stage::render(panel, by_list);

  by_hand = graphics::canvas(64, 32, 0xff000000);
  by_hand.fill_rect(2, 2, 40, 12, 0xff303038);
  by_hand.fill_rect(4, 4, 12, 8, 0xff5060a0);
  assert(canvas_equal(by_list, by_hand), "a panel-shaped list renders through the stage path");
}

tests/facing.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 P5 — facings, and the two models the PROJECTION picks between.
//
// The claim under test is that an author writes ONE call — "this thing points
// that way" — and the stage decides whether that is a rotation or a sequence
// plus a mirror.  Top-down rotates one sprite continuously and must never cost
// an atlas cell for doing it; side-on cannot rotate a standing sprite into
// another facing, so it picks from an (action, facing) table and mirrors at
// most, and a facing change mid-walk must not snap the legs back to frame 0.
use stage;

EPS = 0.000001;
fn near(a: float, b: float) -> boolean { a - b < EPS and b - a < EPS }
fn nears(a: single, b: float) -> boolean {
  d = (a as float) - b;
  d < EPS and -d < EPS
}

// A mob: 10 wide, 20 tall, origin at the FEET (bottom-centre), stood at (100,200).
fn mob(st: stage::Stage, key: text) -> integer {
  st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_ox: 5.0, pl_oy: 20.0,
                                 pl_x: 100.0, pl_y: 200.0, pl_key: key })
}

// ── the quantiser ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// Screen space, y DOWN: angle 0 is +x (east), +pi/2 is +y (south).  Hand-computed
// at every boundary, and the edges are HALF-OPEN like A4's picking — 45 degrees
// belongs to south, not to east, so two facings never both claim one angle.
fn test_the_compass_is_half_open_at_its_boundaries() {
  cases = [0.0, 44.0, 45.0, 90.0, 135.0, 180.0, 225.0, 270.0, 315.0, 359.0,
           -45.0, -46.0, -90.0];
  want  = ["e", "e", "s", "s",   "w",   "w",   "n",   "n",   "e",   "e",
           "e",   "n",   "n"];
  for i in 0..len(cases) {
    d = cases[i] ?? 0.0;
    got = stage::facing_name(d * PI / 180.0);
    assert(got == (want[i] ?? ""), "{d} degrees: got '{got}', want '{want[i] ?? ""}'");
  }
}

// ── TOP-DOWN: continuous rotation, and no atlas cost for it ──────────────────

// ⚠ 15-DEGREE STEPS COST NO ATLAS ENTRIES.  Pre-rotated frames are exactly what
// this model avoids, so 24 facings must be ONE cell turned 24 ways — not 24
// cells.  A sprite authored in a locked orientation (crawler's "front = up") is
// what makes that legal.
fn test_fifteen_degree_steps_cost_no_atlas_entries() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::TOP_DOWN);
  q = st.add_named_sequence("orc", 3, 1, 0.0, stage::LOOP);
  ns: vector<integer> = [];
  for i in 0..24 {
    n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 1.0, pl_h: 1.0, pl_seq: q });
    st.face(n, (i as float) * 15.0 * PI / 180.0);
    ns += [n];
  }
  st.compose();
  assert(st.sequence_count() == 1, "24 facings, one sequence — got {st.sequence_count()}");
  f = st.pack_instances();
  // Every instance names the SAME cell and the same atlas...
  for i in 0..24 {
    base = i * stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE;
    assert(nears(f[base + stage::OFF_CELL] ?? 0.0f, 3.0),
           "facing {i}: cell moved to {f[base + stage::OFF_CELL] ?? 0.0f}");
  }
  // ...and the affines are 24 DISTINCT rotations.  Hand-computed at 15 degrees:
  // cos = 0.9659258, sin = 0.2588190.
  assert(nears(f[stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE] ?? 0.0f, 0.9659258),
         "a at 15 degrees");
  assert(nears(f[stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE + 1] ?? 0.0f, 0.2588190),
         "b at 15 degrees");
  seen = 0;
  for i in 0..24 {
    ai = f[i * stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE] ?? 0.0f;
    bi = f[i * stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE + 1] ?? 0.0f;
    dup = false;
    for j in 0..i {
      aj = f[j * stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE] ?? 0.0f;
      bj = f[j * stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE + stage::OFF_AFFINE + 1] ?? 0.0f;
      if nears(ai, aj as float) and nears(bi, bj as float) { dup = true; }
    }
    if !dup { seen = seen + 1; }
  }
  assert(seen == 24, "24 facings must give 24 distinct affines — got {seen}");
}

// Top-down keeps the angle exactly: it is a rotation, not a bucket.
fn test_top_down_rotation_is_continuous() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::TOP_DOWN);
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.face(n, 0.3);
  st.compose();
  assert(near(st.facing_of(n), 0.3), "the facing is kept exactly");
  assert(near(st.rotation_of(n), 0.3), "and top-down turns the sprite by it");
  st.face(n, 0.31);
  st.compose();
  assert(near(st.rotation_of(n), 0.31), "a hundredth of a radian still moves it");
}

// ── SIDE-ON: an (action, facing) table, mirrored at most ─────────────────────

// ⚠ A FACING CHANGE MID-WALK KEEPS THE PHASE.  Switching sequences must carry the
// elapsed over, or a mob turning a corner snaps its legs back to frame 0 and
// visibly stutters at exactly the moment the player is watching it.
fn test_turning_mid_walk_keeps_the_frame_phase() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_e", 100, 6, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_n", 200, 6, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.set_action(n, "walk");
  st.face(n, 0.0);                       // east
  st.compose();
  st.advance(250000);                    // 0.25 s at 10 fps -> offset 2
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 102, "walking east, on the third cell");
  st.face(n, -PI / 2.0);                 // turn north, mid-stride
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 202,
         "the phase carries over — got {st.frame_of(n)}, want 202 (not 200)");
  st.advance(100000);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 203, "and it keeps walking from there");
}

// ⚠ WEST IS EAST MIRRORED, NOT EAST ROTATED.  A standing sprite turned by pi
// lies on its head; the only legal transform between the two sides is a flip.
// Hand-computed for w=10 h=20 ox=5 oy=20 at (100,200): east packs a=+10, tx=95;
// west packs a=-10, tx=105 — and BOTH span x 95..105 with the origin at
// (100,200).  The footprint does not move, exactly as P3's sway does not.
fn test_west_is_east_mirrored_and_the_footprint_does_not_move() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_e", 100, 6, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.set_action(n, "walk");

  st.face(n, 0.0);                       // east
  st.compose();
  be = st.bounds_of(n);
  oe = st.world_origin(n);
  fe = st.pack_instances();
  assert(nears(fe[stage::OFF_AFFINE] ?? 0.0f, 10.0), "east: a = +w");
  assert(!st.mirrored(n), "east is not mirrored");

  st.face(n, PI);                        // west
  st.compose();
  bw = st.bounds_of(n);
  ow = st.world_origin(n);
  fw = st.pack_instances();
  assert(st.mirrored(n), "west mirrors");
  assert(near(st.rotation_of(n), 0.0),
         "and it does NOT rotate — a standing sprite stays upright");
  assert(nears(fw[stage::OFF_AFFINE] ?? 0.0f, -10.0), "west: a = -w, the flip");
  assert(nears(fw[stage::OFF_AFFINE + 3] ?? 0.0f, 20.0),
         "while d is untouched — flipped side to side, never top to bottom");
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 100, "west reuses the EAST cells, mirrored");
  assert(near(bw.b_x0, be.b_x0) and near(bw.b_x1, be.b_x1),
         "the footprint does not move: {bw.b_x0}..{bw.b_x1} vs {be.b_x0}..{be.b_x1}");
  assert(near(bw.b_y0, be.b_y0) and near(bw.b_y1, be.b_y1), "nor vertically");
  assert(near(ow.p_x, oe.p_x) and near(ow.p_y, oe.p_y),
         "and the origin is still where the mob stands");
}

// North and south have no mirror partner — a front view flipped is still a front
// view — so they fall back rather than borrowing the other one.
fn test_north_and_south_do_not_mirror() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk", 50, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_e", 100, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.set_action(n, "walk");
  st.face(n, -PI / 2.0);                 // north
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 50, "north falls back to the actionful sequence");
  assert(!st.mirrored(n), "and does not borrow east");
}

// ── the projection picks, not the author ─────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ THE SAME CALL, TWO MODELS.  This is the phase's whole claim: `face` means
// "point that way", and what that costs is the projection's decision.
fn test_one_call_two_models() {
  top = stage::stage_new();
  top.set_projection(stage::TOP_DOWN);
  _ = top.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_e", 100, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  tn = mob(top, "orc");
  top.set_action(tn, "walk");
  top.face(tn, PI);
  top.compose();

  side = stage::stage_new();
  side.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  _ = side.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_e", 100, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  sn = mob(side, "orc");
  side.set_action(sn, "walk");
  side.face(sn, PI);
  side.compose();

  assert(near(top.rotation_of(tn), PI), "top-down turns the sprite");
  assert(!top.mirrored(tn), "and never mirrors it");
  assert(near(side.rotation_of(sn), 0.0), "side-on leaves it upright");
  assert(side.mirrored(sn), "and flips it instead");
}

// ── by-name resolution: fall back, never fail ────────────────────────────────

// Most specific wins, and each rung is reached only when the one above is absent.
fn test_resolution_prefers_the_most_specific_name() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.set_action(n, "walk");

  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc", 10, 1, 0.0, stage::LOOP);
  st.face(n, -PI / 2.0);                 // north
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 10, "only the bare key exists, so it is used");

  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk", 20, 1, 0.0, stage::LOOP);
  st.face(n, -PI / 2.0);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 20, "the action variant outranks the bare key");

  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_n", 30, 1, 0.0, stage::LOOP);
  st.face(n, -PI / 2.0);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 30, "and the facing variant outranks that");
}

// ⚠ A MISS FALLS BACK, IT DOES NOT FAIL.  A missing sprite must leave a mob
// drawn with what it had rather than blanking it or stopping the frame — the
// same rule crawler's `<key>.png` loader keeps.
fn test_an_unknown_key_falls_back_rather_than_failing() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_e", 100, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.set_action(n, "walk");
  st.face(n, 0.0);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 100, "resolved east");
  st.set_action(n, "cast_a_spell_nobody_drew");
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 100, "an unknown action keeps what it had");
  st.compose();
  assert(len(st.pack_instances()) == stage::INSTANCE_STRIDE, "and still draws");
}

// ── the degenerate case ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// A flat 2-D game never calls any of this, and nothing above may reach it.
fn test_a_flat_scene_is_untouched() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_x: 3.0, pl_y: 4.0 });
  st.compose();
  before = st.pack_instances();
  assert(st.projection() == stage::TOP_DOWN, "top-down is the default");
  assert(!st.mirrored(n), "nothing is mirrored");
  assert(near(st.facing_of(n), 0.0), "and nothing is facing anywhere");
  after = st.pack_instances();
  assert(len(before) == len(after), "reading it changed nothing");
  for i in 0..len(before) {
    assert((before[i] ?? 0.0f) == (after[i] ?? 0.0f), "float {i} moved");
  }
}

// ⚠ A NAME BELONGS TO ITS OWN SEQUENCE.  This was a real defect: the name lived
// in a vector beside `st_seqs` that only the naming registrar appended to, so
// mixing `add_sequence` with `add_named_sequence` shifted every name onto
// somebody else's cells — silently, because both lists stayed individually
// well-formed.  "orc" resolved to cell 900.
fn test_mixing_registrars_keeps_names_on_their_own_sequence() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  _ = st.add_sequence(900, 1, 0.0, stage::LOOP);             // unnamed, index 0
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc", 42, 1, 0.0, stage::LOOP);  // named,   index 1
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.face(n, 0.0);
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 42, "'orc' must name cell 42 — got {st.frame_of(n)}");
  assert(st.sequence_count() == 2, "both registrars add exactly one sequence");
}

// Changing the projection changes what the NEXT `face` means — the model is the
// stage's, so a mob that keeps facing each frame lands in the new one by itself.
// (The projection belongs at setup; this is what happens if it moves anyway.)
fn test_the_projection_decides_the_next_face() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.add_named_sequence("orc_walk_e", 100, 4, 10.0, stage::LOOP);
  n = mob(st, "orc");
  st.set_action(n, "walk");
  st.face(n, PI);                        // top-down by default: turns
  assert(near(st.rotation_of(n), PI), "top-down turned it");
  assert(!st.mirrored(n), "and did not flip it");

  st.set_projection(stage::SIDE_ON);
  st.face(n, PI);                        // the same call, now the other model
  assert(st.mirrored(n), "side-on flips it instead");
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 100, "onto the east cells, mirrored");
}

tests/light.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 L1 — light sampled per sprite, applied as tint.
//
// The whole feature for a flat-lit 2-D game, and deliberately first: it rides
// A5's tint attribute, so it costs no pass, needs no framebuffer, and cannot
// disturb draw order.  L2 is only worth its pass when light must fall ACROSS the
// scene rather than across the sprites.
use stage;
use graphics;

EPS = 0.000001;
fn near(a: float, b: float) -> boolean { a - b < EPS and b - a < EPS }
fn count_of(cv: const graphics::Canvas, want: integer) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v == want { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}

// A mob standing at (x, y): 10 wide, 20 tall, origin at its FEET.
fn mob_at(st: stage::Stage, x: float, y: float) -> integer {
  st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_ox: 5.0, pl_oy: 20.0,
                                 pl_x: x, pl_y: y, pl_colour: 0xc03030 })
}

// ── the degenerate case first ────────────────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ AN UNLIT SCENE IS UNTOUCHED.  Ambient is 1.0 and there are no lights, so a
// game that never asks for light cannot be dimmed by this feature existing.
fn test_no_lights_is_the_material_unchanged() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = mob_at(st, 100.0, 200.0);
  st.compose();
  assert(near(st.ambient(), 1.0), "ambient starts fully lit");
  assert(st.light_count() == 0, "and there are no lights");
  assert(st.lit_colour(n) == 0xc03030, "so the colour is the material, exactly");
}

// Dimming with no lights at all is the other degenerate case, and it is how a
// night scene starts before a torch is put in it.
fn test_ambient_alone_dims_everything_evenly() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  a = mob_at(st, 0.0, 0.0);
  b = mob_at(st, 900.0, 900.0);
  st.set_ambient(0.5);
  st.compose();
  // 0xC03030 = (192,48,48); halved and rounded = (96,24,24).
  assert(st.lit_colour(a) == 0x601818, "half light, hand-computed");
  assert(st.lit_colour(b) == 0x601818, "and distance means nothing without a light");
}

// ── falloff, every value worked out by hand ──────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ THE FALLOFF IS LINEAR IN THE RADIUS — `1 - d/r`, clamped.  Chosen because
// this phase's gate is hand-computed values, and a curve nobody can hand-compute
// is a gate nobody maintains.  L2 owns the question of a prettier curve, and its
// own gate says so: expectations GENERATED from the constants the shader uses.
fn test_the_falloff_is_hand_computed_at_every_distance() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  _ = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  on   = mob_at(st, 100.0, 200.0);   // d = 0
  half = mob_at(st, 150.0, 200.0);   // d = 50
  edge = mob_at(st, 200.0, 200.0);   // d = 100, exactly the radius
  past = mob_at(st, 250.0, 200.0);   // d = 150
  st.compose();
  assert(st.lit_colour(on) == 0xc03030, "standing on the light: the full material");
  assert(st.lit_colour(half) == 0x601818, "half way out: exactly half");
  assert(st.lit_colour(edge) == 0x000000, "at the radius the light has ended");
  assert(st.lit_colour(past) == 0x000000, "and beyond it stays ended, never negative");
}

// Equal distance, equal light — in any direction.  A falloff that used only x,
// or the bounding box, passes the horizontal cell and fails this one.
fn test_two_sprites_at_equal_distance_take_equal_tint() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  _ = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  east  = mob_at(st, 150.0, 200.0);
  west  = mob_at(st, 50.0, 200.0);
  south = mob_at(st, 100.0, 250.0);
  north = mob_at(st, 100.0, 150.0);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.lit_colour(east) == 0x601818, "east");
  assert(st.lit_colour(west) == st.lit_colour(east), "west matches");
  assert(st.lit_colour(south) == st.lit_colour(east), "south matches");
  assert(st.lit_colour(north) == st.lit_colour(east), "north matches");
}

// ── light COMPOSES with the material, it does not replace it ─────────────────

// ⚠ A RED SPRITE UNDER A BLUE LIGHT GOES DARK, NOT BLUE.  Light multiplies the
// material — overwriting it would throw away A5's tint, which is the attribute
// this rides.  Hand-computed: (192,48,48) x (0,0,1) = (0,0,48).
fn test_light_composes_with_the_material_tint() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  _ = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0x0000ff, 1.0);
  n = mob_at(st, 100.0, 200.0);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.lit_colour(n) == 0x000030,
         "red under blue is nearly black — got {st.lit_colour(n)}");
}

// ⚠ TWO LIGHTS CANNOT OVERFLOW PAST THE MATERIAL.  Unclamped, 2.0 x 192 wraps
// and a doubly-lit sprite comes out DARK — the classic brightness bug, and the
// one everyone finds by standing between two torches.
fn test_overlapping_lights_clamp_instead_of_wrapping() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  _ = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  _ = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  n = mob_at(st, 100.0, 200.0);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.lit_colour(n) == 0xc03030,
         "two full lights is the material, not more and not wrapped");
}

// ── sampled at the ORIGIN — the footprint, not the artwork ───────────────────

// ⚠ THE SAMPLE POINT IS THE SPRITE'S ORIGIN, the same point P1 sorts on.  Two
// mobs standing on one tile must take the same light however tall their artwork
// is; sample the corner instead and a tall sprite is lit as though it stood
// further away, which is the 2.5-D wrong picture in another form.
fn test_light_is_sampled_at_the_footprint_not_the_artwork() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  _ = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  short = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_ox: 5.0,
                                         pl_oy: 20.0, pl_x: 150.0, pl_y: 200.0,
                                         pl_colour: 0xc03030 });
  tall  = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 60.0, pl_ox: 5.0,
                                         pl_oy: 60.0, pl_x: 150.0, pl_y: 200.0,
                                         pl_colour: 0xc03030 });
  st.compose();
  o1 = st.world_origin(short);
  o2 = st.world_origin(tall);
  assert(near(o1.p_x, o2.p_x) and near(o1.p_y, o2.p_y),
         "both stand on the same spot, whatever their height");
  assert(st.lit_colour(short) == 0x601818, "the short one, hand-computed at d=50");
  assert(st.lit_colour(tall) == st.lit_colour(short),
         "and the tall one takes exactly the same light");
}

// ── it rides A5's attribute: no pass, no reorder ─────────────────────────────

// ⚠ LIGHT MUST NOT DISTURB THE DRAW.  It is a colour, not a pass — so the batch
// count, the draw order and the geometry are all unchanged by lighting a scene.
fn test_light_changes_no_batch_and_no_order() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = mob_at(st, 100.0, 200.0);
  _ = mob_at(st, 140.0, 200.0);
  _ = mob_at(st, 180.0, 200.0);
  st.compose();
  runs_before = len(st.batches());
  order_before = "";
  for i in st.order() { order_before += "{i},"; }

  st.set_ambient(0.2);
  _ = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 90.0, 0xffe0a0, 1.0);
  st.compose();
  order_after = "";
  for i in st.order() { order_after += "{i},"; }
  assert(len(st.batches()) == runs_before,
         "lighting adds no run — {len(st.batches())} vs {runs_before}");
  assert(order_after == order_before, "and reorders nothing");
}

// ⚠ BUT IT DOES DIRTY THE BUFFER, unlike the camera.  A light changes what is
// IN the instance data, so honesty here matters: a moving torch re-packs every
// frame, and that is exactly the cost L2's pass exists to take over.
fn test_a_light_dirties_the_buffer_where_a_camera_does_not() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = mob_at(st, 100.0, 200.0);
  st.compose();
  _ = st.sync_instances();
  st.set_camera(50.0, 0.0);
  assert(!st.sync_instances(), "a camera move re-packs nothing");
  l = st.add_light(100.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  assert(st.sync_instances(), "adding a light does re-pack");
  st.move_light(l, 120.0, 200.0);
  assert(st.sync_instances(), "and so does moving one");
  st.set_ambient(0.5);
  assert(st.sync_instances(), "and changing the ambient");
}

// ── one home: both render paths agree ────────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ THE LIT COLOUR IS DERIVED ONCE and both paths read it.  P6 shipped a defect
// that was exactly this shape the other way round — a fact re-derived per path
// and validated only where the derivations happened to coincide.
fn test_both_render_paths_agree_about_the_light() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  // ⚠ THE LIGHT IS PLACED SO THE LIT COLOUR DIFFERS FROM THE MATERIAL.  An
  // earlier version stood the sprite ON the light, where the two are equal — so
  // a render path that ignored lighting entirely still matched, and the test
  // proved nothing.  The control is what exposed that; the assertion below now
  // makes it impossible to drift back.
  _ = st.add_light(52.0, 3.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);   // d = 50, reach 0.5
  n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 2.0, pl_y: 3.0, pl_w: 5.0, pl_h: 4.0,
                                     pl_colour: 0xc03030 });
  st.compose();
  want = st.lit_colour(n);
  assert(want == 0x601818, "half-lit, hand-computed — got {want}");
  assert(want != 0xc03030, "and it is NOT the raw material, or this proves nothing");
  cv = graphics::canvas(16, 16, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  assert(count_of(cv, 0xff000000 | want) == 20,
         "the software path paints the LIT colour over its 5x4");

  f = st.pack_instances();
  // The packed rgba is premultiplied and alpha is 1, so it is the lit colour
  // divided by 255 — the same number the software path used.
  r = ((f[stage::OFF_RGBA] ?? 0.0f) as float) * 255.0 + 0.5;
  assert((r as integer) == ((want >> 16) & 255),
         "and the instance buffer carries the same red: {r as integer}");
}

// ⚠ A LIGHT OUT OF RANGE CONTRIBUTES NOTHING — it must not SUBTRACT.  Unclamped,
// `1 - d/r` goes negative past the radius, and a distant lamp then darkens what
// a nearby one lit: the sprite at 250 is half-lit by B and 250 units outside A,
// where the raw falloff is -4.  With one light the final clamp hides it, so this
// is the cell that catches it — and the control that removed the clamp passed
// every other test in this file.
fn test_a_light_out_of_range_does_not_subtract() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  _ = st.add_light(0.0, 200.0, 50.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);     // far away, tiny radius
  _ = st.add_light(300.0, 200.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);  // the one that lights it
  n = mob_at(st, 250.0, 200.0);                          // d = 250 from A, 50 from B
  st.compose();
  assert(st.lit_colour(n) == 0x601818,
         "lit by B alone, undimmed by A — got {st.lit_colour(n)}");
}

// ⚠ THE REACH ITSELF IS BOUNDED, and this is where that is gated — `lit_colour`
// skips a zero contribution as work avoided, so a negative reach would never
// show through it.  The invariant is `light_reach`'s, so it is tested on
// `light_reach`: past the radius it answers exactly 0, never a negative that
// would let a distant lamp DARKEN what a near one lit.
fn test_the_reach_is_bounded_at_both_ends() {
  l = stage::StageLight { lt_x: 0.0, lt_y: 0.0, lt_r: 100.0,
                          lt_colour: 0xffffff, lt_power: 1.0 };
  assert(near(stage::light_reach(l, 0.0, 0.0), 1.0), "at the centre, full");
  assert(near(stage::light_reach(l, 50.0, 0.0), 0.5), "half way, half");
  assert(near(stage::light_reach(l, 100.0, 0.0), 0.0), "at the radius, nothing");
  assert(near(stage::light_reach(l, 500.0, 0.0), 0.0),
         "and far beyond it is still nothing, NOT -4: got {stage::light_reach(l, 500.0, 0.0)}");
  z = stage::StageLight { lt_x: 0.0, lt_y: 0.0, lt_r: 0.0,
                          lt_colour: 0xffffff, lt_power: 1.0 };
  assert(near(stage::light_reach(z, 0.0, 0.0), 0.0),
         "a radius of zero reaches nothing, and does not divide by it");
}

tests/lightmap.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 L2 — the light-map composite pass.
//
// What L1 cannot do: light that falls ACROSS the scene rather than across the
// sprites.  The world draws, one multiply composite lights every pixel of it
// including the gaps between sprites, and the HUD draws after, unlit.
//
// ⚠ THE EXPECTATIONS ARE GENERATED, NOT TYPED.  A curve hand-computed per pixel
// is not a gate anyone maintains, so these read the falloff from the SAME symbol
// the renderer and the shader use.  What is hand-computed is the floor, the
// ceiling and the shape — monotonic, bounded, and never under its floor.
use stage;
use graphics;

EPS = 0.000001;
fn near(a: float, b: float) -> boolean { a - b < EPS and b - a < EPS }
fn red_of(cv: const graphics::Canvas, x: integer, y: integer) -> integer {
  (cv.get_pixel(x, y) >> 16) & 255
}
fn differing(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if (a.data[i] ?? 0) != (b.data[i] ?? 0) { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}

// A white slab covering the whole canvas, so a pixel's red channel reads the
// light level directly.
fn slab(st: stage::Stage, layer: integer) -> integer {
  st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 200.0, pl_h: 40.0, pl_colour: 0xffffff,
                                 pl_layer: layer })
}

// ── the degenerate case ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ THE MAP IS OFF UNTIL ASKED.  With it off the scene is exactly what L1 drew,
// byte for byte — so a game that wants light across its SPRITES keeps it.
fn test_the_map_is_off_by_default_and_l1_is_untouched() {
  a = stage::stage_new();
  a.set_ambient(0.5);
  _ = a.add_light(50.0, 20.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  _ = slab(a, 0);
  a.compose();
  assert(!a.light_map(), "off by default");
  ca = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  a.render_view(0, ca);

  b = stage::stage_new();
  b.set_ambient(0.5);
  _ = b.add_light(50.0, 20.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  _ = slab(b, 0);
  b.compose();
  cb = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  b.render_view(0, cb);
  assert(differing(ca, cb) == 0, "two unlit-map stages agree");

  // And turning it on must actually CHANGE something, or this proves nothing.
  b.set_light_map(true);
  cc = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  b.render_view(0, cc);
  assert(differing(ca, cc) > 0, "the map is not a no-op");
}

// ── the point of the phase: light falls ACROSS a sprite ──────────────────────

// ⚠ THIS IS WHAT L1 CANNOT DO.  One sprite, one light near its left end: the
// near end is brighter than the far end.  Per-sprite lighting gives the whole
// slab ONE value, so it fails this by construction.
fn test_light_falls_across_a_single_sprite() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  st.set_light_map(true);
  _ = st.add_light(0.0, 20.0, 200.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  _ = slab(st, 0);
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(0, cv);
  near_end = red_of(cv, 0, 20);
  mid     = red_of(cv, 100, 20);
  far_end = red_of(cv, 199, 20);
  assert(near_end > mid and mid > far_end,
         "one sprite, a gradient across it: {near_end} > {mid} > {far_end}");
  assert(near_end == 255, "at the light itself, full");
}

// ⚠ AND IT LIGHTS THE GAPS.  A fullscreen composite reaches the background
// between sprites, which is the reason it earns a pass at all — a per-sprite
// tint cannot touch a pixel no sprite covers.
fn test_the_composite_lights_the_background_too() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.0);
  st.set_light_map(true);
  _ = st.add_light(10.0, 20.0, 200.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  // A small sprite, so most of the canvas is bare background.
  _ = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_colour: 0xffffff,
                                     pl_x: 150.0, pl_y: 30.0 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff404040);
  st.render_view(0, cv);
  assert(red_of(cv, 12, 20) > red_of(cv, 190, 20),
         "bare background is lit, and falls off with distance");
}

// ── the invariant: monotonic, and never under its floor ──────────────────────

// ⚠ THE GATE THE PLAN ASKS FOR, and it is a PROPERTY rather than a table:
// along a ray from the light the level never rises, and never drops below the
// floor the ambient sets.  Hand-computing a curve per pixel is not a gate
// anyone maintains; this is.
fn test_the_falloff_along_a_ray_is_monotonic_and_floored() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  floor = 0.25;
  st.set_ambient(floor);
  st.set_light_map(true);
  _ = st.add_light(0.0, 0.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  prev = 2.0;
  for i in 0..40 {
    d = (i as float) * 5.0;            // out to 195, well past the radius
    lv = st.light_level_at(d, 0.0);
    assert(lv <= prev + EPS, "step {i} at d={d} rose: {lv} after {prev}");
    assert(lv >= floor - EPS, "step {i} at d={d} went under the floor: {lv}");
    assert(lv <= 1.0 + EPS, "step {i} at d={d} went over the ceiling: {lv}");
    prev = lv;
  }
  assert(near(st.light_level_at(500.0, 0.0), floor),
         "far away it IS the floor, exactly");
  assert(near(st.light_level_at(0.0, 0.0), 1.0),
         "and at the light it is the ceiling");
}

// ⚠ GENERATED FROM THE SAME SYMBOL THE RENDERER USES.  Not a second table of
// numbers to keep in step — if the curve is retuned, this test retunes with it,
// which is the whole point of the technique.
fn test_the_level_agrees_with_the_shared_reach_function() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.1);
  st.set_light_map(true);
  l = stage::StageLight { lt_x: 30.0, lt_y: 40.0, lt_r: 120.0,
                          lt_colour: 0xffffff, lt_power: 1.0 };
  _ = st.add_light(l.lt_x, l.lt_y, l.lt_r, l.lt_colour, l.lt_power);
  for i in 0..12 {
    px = (i as float) * 13.0;
    want = 0.1 + stage::light_reach(l, px, 40.0);
    if want > 1.0 { want = 1.0; }
    got = st.light_level_at(px, 40.0);
    assert(near(got, want), "x={px}: composite {got} vs reach {want}");
  }
}

// ── the HUD draws after, unlit ───────────────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ THE COMMENT BECOMES A TEST.  "The HUD draws after, unlit" is only true if
// its pixels are BIT-IDENTICAL with the light on and off — anything less and a
// dark room quietly dims the health bar.
fn test_the_hud_is_bit_identical_with_the_light_on_and_off() {
  lit = stage::stage_new();
  lit.set_hud_layer(10);
  lit.set_light_map(true);
  lit.set_ambient(0.0);
  _ = lit.add_light(20.0, 20.0, 60.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  _ = slab(lit, 0);                                     // world
  _ = lit.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_x: 100.0,
                                      pl_y: 5.0, pl_colour: 0x40e080,
                                      pl_layer: 10 });  // HUD
  lit.compose();
  ca = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  lit.render_view(0, ca);

  dark = stage::stage_new();
  dark.set_hud_layer(10);
  dark.set_light_map(true);
  dark.set_ambient(0.0);                                // NO light at all
  _ = slab(dark, 0);
  _ = dark.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_x: 100.0,
                                       pl_y: 5.0, pl_colour: 0x40e080,
                                       pl_layer: 10 });
  dark.compose();
  cb = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  dark.render_view(0, cb);

  // The world differs — one is lit, one is not.
  assert(ca.get_pixel(20, 30) != cb.get_pixel(20, 30), "the world DID change");
  // The HUD does not, anywhere in its rect.
  moved = 0;
  for y in 5..15 {
    for x in 100..120 {
      if ca.get_pixel(x, y) != cb.get_pixel(x, y) { moved = moved + 1; }
    }
  }
  assert(moved == 0, "{moved} HUD pixels moved when the light changed");
  assert(ca.get_pixel(105, 8) == 0xff40e080, "and the HUD is its own colour, undimmed");
}

// ── the two models do not stack ──────────────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ ONE LIGHTING MODEL AT A TIME.  With the map on, a sprite is packed with its
// RAW material and the map does the lighting — otherwise every sprite would be
// lit twice and a torch-lit mob would come out darker than the floor it stands on.
fn test_the_map_replaces_per_sprite_lighting() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  st.set_ambient(0.5);
  _ = st.add_light(0.0, 0.0, 100.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0, pl_colour: 0xc03030,
                                     pl_x: 60.0, pl_y: 0.0 });
  st.compose();
  assert(st.lit_colour(n) != 0xc03030, "with the map OFF, L1 tints the sprite");
  st.set_light_map(true);
  assert(st.lit_colour(n) == 0xc03030,
         "with the map ON the sprite is raw material — got {st.lit_colour(n)}");
}

// ── order independence ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// A multiply composite after the scene cannot depend on the order the scene was
// drawn in, so it does not fight A3a's *never reorder*.
fn test_the_composite_is_order_independent() {
  one = stage::stage_new();
  one.set_light_map(true);
  one.set_ambient(0.2);
  _ = one.add_light(30.0, 20.0, 120.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  _ = one.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 60.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_x: 0.0,
                                      pl_y: 10.0, pl_colour: 0xffffff, pl_depth: 1.0 });
  _ = one.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 60.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_x: 40.0,
                                      pl_y: 10.0, pl_colour: 0x8080ff, pl_depth: 0.0 });
  one.compose();
  c1 = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  one.render_view(0, c1);

  two = stage::stage_new();          // the same scene, inserted the other way up
  two.set_light_map(true);
  two.set_ambient(0.2);
  _ = two.add_light(30.0, 20.0, 120.0, 0xffffff, 1.0);
  _ = two.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 60.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_x: 40.0,
                                      pl_y: 10.0, pl_colour: 0x8080ff, pl_depth: 0.0 });
  _ = two.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 60.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_x: 0.0,
                                      pl_y: 10.0, pl_colour: 0xffffff, pl_depth: 1.0 });
  two.compose();
  c2 = graphics::canvas(200, 40, 0xff000000);
  two.render_view(0, c2);
  assert(differing(c1, c2) == 0, "{differing(c1, c2)} pixels depended on insertion order");
}

// ── the shader reads the same constant ───────────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ ONE HOME FOR THE CURVE.  The composite shader takes the falloff as a
// UNIFORM fed from `LIGHT_FALLOFF` — the same symbol `light_reach` uses — rather
// than a number baked into its text.  Baking it would be a second copy, and
// interpolating it is worse than it looks: loft renders `2.0` as `2`, which GLSL
// takes as an int and refuses.
fn test_the_composite_shader_takes_the_falloff_as_a_uniform() {
  src = stage::composite_shader_source();
  assert(src.contains("uniform float uFalloff"), "the shader declares the uniform");
  assert(src.contains("uniform float uFloor"), "and the floor it must never undercut");
  // The exponent the curve is raised to must BE the uniform — a number here
  // would be a second copy of the curve, free to drift from `light_reach`.
  assert(src.contains("uFalloff)"), "the exponent is the uniform, not a literal");
  assert(near(stage::LIGHT_FALLOFF, 1.0), "the curve is linear today");
}

tests/nodetween.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN145 C2 — a tween bound to a node property.
//
// THE GATE is a PARALLEL RUN, the same shape `draw_list.loft` uses for A2: the
// tween-driven scene and a hand-moved one both exist, and are compared pixel for
// pixel every frame rather than one being swapped in and eyeballed.
//
// ⚠ The numbers are chosen so the HAND side needs no library call at all: 64 px
// over 800 000 us sampled every 100 000 us is exactly 8 px per frame, and every
// progress `k/8` is exact in binary.  A hand path that reached for `lerp` or
// `ease` would be comparing the tween against itself.
use stage;
use graphics;

const DUR  = 800000;     // us
const STEP = 100000;     // us — eight frames
const FAR  = 64.0;       // px — so each frame is exactly 8 px

fn canvas_equal(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> boolean {
  if a.width != b.width or a.height != b.height { return false; }
  for i in 0..len(a.data) {
    if a.data[i] != b.data[i] { return false; }
  }
  true
}

fn differing(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if a.data[i] != b.data[i] { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}

fn mover(st: Stage) -> integer {
  st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0, pl_colour: 0xc03030 })
}

fn shot(st: Stage) -> graphics::Canvas {
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(96, 16, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  cv
}

// THE GATE.  Eight frames of a tweened `x`, byte-identical to eight frames of a
// hand-moved one.
fn test_a_tweened_property_draws_what_a_hand_moved_one_draws() {
  auto = stage_new();
  a = mover(auto);
  auto.tween_prop(a, Prop.X, FAR, DUR);

  hand = stage_new();
  h = mover(hand);

  ink = 0;
  for k in 1..=8 {
    auto.advance(STEP);
    // The hand side: k frames of 8 px, computed here and written straight in.
    hand.set_prop(h, Prop.X, (k * 8) as float);

    ca = shot(auto);
    ch = shot(hand);
    assert(canvas_equal(ca, ch),
           "frame {k} differs in {differing(ca, ch)} pixels — tween x is {auto.prop_of(a, Prop.X)}, hand is {hand.prop_of(h, Prop.X)}");
    for v in ca.data { if v == 0xffc03030 { ink = ink + 1; } }
  }
  assert(auto.prop_of(a, Prop.X) == FAR, "and it lands exactly on 64");
  assert(ink == 8 * 64, "each of the 8 frames drew an 8x8 sprite, got {ink}");
}

// ⚠ THE CONTROL for the gate: the comparison can FAIL.  One pixel of offset on
// the hand side and the canvases part — so a green gate above is a statement
// about the values, not about a comparison that never fires.
fn test_the_pixel_comparison_can_fail() {
  a_st = stage_new();
  a_n = mover(a_st);
  a_st.set_prop(a_n, Prop.X, 8.0);
  b_st = stage_new();
  b_n = mover(b_st);
  b_st.set_prop(b_n, Prop.X, 9.0);
  ca = shot(a_st);
  cb = shot(b_st);
  assert(!canvas_equal(ca, cb), "one pixel of offset must show");
  assert(differing(ca, cb) == 16, "an 8x8 sprite moved by 1 px differs in 16 pixels, got {differing(ca, cb)}");
}

// ⚠ THE SWITCH, cell by cell.  Nine properties, and a write to one must move
// that one and NOTHING else — the copy-paste failure a nine-arm match invites.
fn test_each_property_writes_its_own_field_and_no_other() {
  props = [Prop.X, Prop.Y, Prop.Rot, Prop.ScaleX, Prop.ScaleY,
           Prop.OriginX, Prop.OriginY, Prop.Alpha, Prop.Depth];
  assert(len(props) == 9, "nine properties");
  for p in props {
    st = stage_new();
    n = mover(st);
    before: vector<float> = [];
    for q in props { before += [st.prop_of(n, q)]; }

    st.set_prop(n, p, 0.375);          // a value no field holds by default

    changed = 0;
    for q in props {
      now = st.prop_of(n, q);
      was = before[q#index] ?? 0.0;
      if now != was {
        changed = changed + 1;
        assert(q == p, "writing one property moved another one");
        assert(now == 0.375, "and it landed on the value written");
      }
    }
    assert(changed == 1, "exactly one field moved, {changed} did");
  }
}

// ⚠⚠ AT MOST ONE TWEEN PER (NODE, PROPERTY) — a second REPLACES the first, so
// one number never has two writers.
fn test_a_second_tween_replaces_the_first() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = mover(st);
  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.X, 64.0, DUR);
  assert(st.tween_count() == 1, "one tween");
  st.advance(400000);                       // half way: 32
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 32.0, "half way is 32, got {st.prop_of(n, Prop.X)}");

  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.X, 0.0, 400000);    // now go back instead
  assert(st.tween_count() == 1, "still ONE tween — the first was replaced, not joined");
  st.advance(200000);                       // half of the new one: 32 → 16
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 16.0, "the new tween starts from 32, got {st.prop_of(n, Prop.X)}");
  st.advance(200000);
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 0.0, "and lands on its own target");

  // A tween on a DIFFERENT property of the same node is a different writer and
  // coexists.
  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.Y, 10.0, DUR);
  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.X, 5.0, DUR);
  assert(st.tween_count() == 2, "x and y are two tweens, got {st.tween_count()}");
}

// ⚠ A FINISHED TWEEN STOPS WRITING, so a later hand-set sticks instead of being
// re-asserted every frame.
fn test_a_finished_tween_releases_the_property() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = mover(st);
  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.X, 64.0, DUR);
  st.advance(DUR);
  assert(!st.prop_tweening(n, Prop.X), "it has finished");
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 64.0, "on its end value");

  st.set_prop(n, Prop.X, 5.0);
  st.advance(STEP);
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 5.0,
         "the finished tween must not write again — got {st.prop_of(n, Prop.X)}");
}

// `from` is read when the tween STARTS, so one begun mid-flight carries on from
// where the property actually is.
fn test_from_is_read_at_the_moment_it_starts() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = mover(st);
  st.set_prop(n, Prop.X, 20.0);
  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.X, 28.0, DUR);
  st.advance(400000);
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 24.0, "half of 20→28 is 24, got {st.prop_of(n, Prop.X)}");
}

// Cancelling leaves the property where it had got to.
fn test_cancel_leaves_the_value_where_it_is() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = mover(st);
  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.X, 64.0, DUR);
  st.advance(400000);
  st.cancel_prop_tween(n, Prop.X);
  assert(st.tween_count() == 0, "the tween is gone");
  st.advance(DUR);
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 32.0, "and the value stayed at 32");
}

// ⚠ ONE CLOCK.  The same `advance` drives an animation sequence and a property
// tween — which is the whole reason the binding lives here and takes MICROSECONDS
// rather than `tween`'s own unit-agnostic count.
fn test_one_advance_drives_both_the_sequence_and_the_tween() {
  st = stage_new();
  q = st.add_sequence(0, 8, 10.0, LOOP);       // 8 frames at 10 fps = 0.8 s
  n = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 8.0, pl_h: 8.0, pl_seq: q, pl_cols: 4 });
  st.tween_prop(n, Prop.X, 64.0, DUR);         // 0.8 s, the same span

  for _ in 0..4 { st.advance(STEP); }
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 4, "four ticks in, the sequence is on frame 4");
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 32.0, "and the tween is half way");

  for _ in 0..4 { st.advance(STEP); }
  assert(st.frame_of(n) == 0, "a full loop returns to frame 0");
  assert(st.prop_of(n, Prop.X) == 64.0, "and the tween has arrived");
}

// A tween on a node index that does not exist is dropped, not stored.
fn test_a_bad_node_index_is_refused() {
  st = stage_new();
  _ = mover(st);
  st.tween_prop(99, Prop.X, 5.0, DUR);
  st.tween_prop(-1, Prop.X, 5.0, DUR);
  assert(st.tween_count() == 0, "neither was kept");
  assert(st.prop_of(99, Prop.X) == 0.0, "and reading one answers the zero");
}

tests/picking.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 A4 — picking and input routing, on a hand-computed table.
//
// Two things here are not what a rect test gives you, and both are the point: a
// point over a sprite's HOLE resolves to what is behind it, and a release goes to
// the node that took the PRESS wherever the pointer has drifted to.
use stage;

fn solid(x: float, y: float, w: float, h: float) -> stage::Place {
  stage::Place { pl_x: x, pl_y: y, pl_w: w, pl_h: h }
}

// The topmost node wins, and "topmost" means LAST — the same order that draws.
fn test_the_last_node_drawn_is_the_one_picked() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  under = st.node_add(-1, solid(0.0, 0.0, 20.0, 20.0));
  over  = st.node_add(-1, solid(10.0, 10.0, 20.0, 20.0));
  st.compose();
  assert(st.pick(5.0, 5.0) == under, "only the lower node covers (5,5)");
  assert(st.pick(25.0, 25.0) == over, "only the upper node covers (25,25)");
  assert(st.pick(15.0, 15.0) == over, "where they overlap, the LAST one wins");
  assert(st.pick(50.0, 50.0) == -1, "nothing covers (50,50)");
}

// The rect is half-open in the same direction the draw list is: a point exactly on
// the far edge is outside, so abutting nodes never both claim a pixel.
fn test_the_rect_is_half_open() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, solid(0.0, 0.0, 10.0, 10.0));
  b = st.node_add(-1, solid(10.0, 0.0, 10.0, 10.0));
  st.compose();
  assert(st.pick(9.99, 5.0) == a, "just inside a");
  assert(st.pick(10.0, 5.0) == b, "the shared edge belongs to b, not to both");
}

// Under rotation the inverse has to be real.  A quarter turn about the origin:
// the node occupies local x in 0..20, y in 0..10, which in world space is
// x in -10..0, y in 0..20.  So (-5, 5) is inside and (5, 5) is not.
fn test_picking_under_rotation() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 10.0,
                                     pl_rot: 1.5707963267948966 });
  st.compose();
  assert(st.pick(-5.0, 5.0) == n, "inside the rotated rect");
  assert(st.pick(5.0, 5.0) == -1, "outside it — the un-rotated rect would say inside");
  l = st.to_local(n, -5.0, 5.0);
  assert(l.p_x > 4.9 and l.p_x < 5.1 and l.p_y > 4.9 and l.p_y < 5.1,
         "to_local inverts the affine: got ({l.p_x}, {l.p_y}) want (5, 5)");
}

// ⚠ THE ONE A RECT TEST CANNOT DO.  A mask with a hole in the middle: a click
// there falls THROUGH to the node behind, because you can see through it.
fn test_a_click_falls_through_a_hole() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  behind = st.node_add(-1, solid(0.0, 0.0, 40.0, 40.0));
  // 2x2 mask: opaque except the bottom-right quadrant.
  holed = st.add_mask(2, 2, [255, 255, 255, 0]);
  front = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 40.0, pl_h: 40.0, pl_mask: holed });
  st.compose();
  assert(st.pick(5.0, 5.0) == front, "the opaque quadrant belongs to the front node");
  assert(st.pick(35.0, 35.0) == behind, "the HOLE lets the click reach what is behind");
  assert(st.pick(35.0, 5.0) == front, "top-right is opaque");
}

// A node with no mask is solid — the right default for a panel or a tile.
fn test_no_mask_means_solid() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, solid(0.0, 0.0, 10.0, 10.0));
  st.compose();
  assert(st.pick(1.0, 1.0) == n and st.pick(9.0, 9.0) == n, "every point inside hits");
}

// ⚠ CAPTURE.  Press on a node, drift off it, release: the release still belongs
// to the node that was pressed.  Without this a button fires when you press and
// slide away, and never fires when you press and drift a pixel.
fn test_a_release_belongs_to_the_press() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  btn = st.node_add(-1, solid(0.0, 0.0, 10.0, 10.0));
  st.compose();
  assert(st.press(5.0, 5.0) == btn, "the press lands on the button");
  assert(st.capture() == btn, "and is held");
  assert(st.release(500.0, 500.0) == btn, "the release belongs to it even far away");
  assert(st.capture() == -1, "and the hold is let go");
  assert(!st.released_on(btn, 500.0, 500.0), "but it did NOT release ON the button");
  assert(st.press(5.0, 5.0) == btn and st.released_on(btn, 6.0, 6.0),
         "pressed and released on it — this is the one that fires");
}

// A press that hits nothing captures nothing, and its release says so.
fn test_a_press_on_nothing_captures_nothing() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = st.node_add(-1, solid(0.0, 0.0, 10.0, 10.0));
  st.compose();
  assert(st.press(50.0, 50.0) == -1, "the press misses");
  assert(st.capture() == -1, "nothing is held");
  assert(st.release(5.0, 5.0) == -1, "and the release reports nothing, not the node under it");
}

tests/stage.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 A1 — transform composition, checked against HAND-COMPUTED POINTS.
//
// Not against matrix entries.  `mesh3d` has no `mat4_rotate_z`, so a mat4 oracle
// cannot even express a 2-D rotation — but the deeper reason is that comparing
// entries checks a convention while comparing transformed points checks the
// thing a renderer needs.  Every expected value below is worked out by hand and
// written as a literal; agreement between two runs of the same code is not a pass.
use stage;

EPS = 0.0001;

fn near(a: float, b: float) -> boolean { a - b < EPS and b - a < EPS }

fn check_pt(name: text, got: stage::P2, ex: float, ey: float) {
    assert(near(got.p_x, ex) and near(got.p_y, ey),
           "{name}: got ({got.p_x}, {got.p_y}) want ({ex}, {ey})");
}

// The ORIGIN is the anchor: local (ox, oy) must land exactly on (x, y), whatever
// the rotation and scale are.  This is what lets a sprite stand up from its feet.
fn test_origin_is_the_anchor() {
    st = stage::stage_new();
    n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 100.0, pl_y: 40.0, pl_rot: 1.1, pl_sx: 3.0, pl_sy: 0.5, pl_ox: 5.0, pl_oy: 9.0 });
    st.compose();
    check_pt("origin lands on (x,y)", st.world_origin(n), 100.0, 40.0);
}

// A quarter turn: +ve rotation takes +x toward +y.  With the origin at (0,0) and
// unit scale, local (1,0) -> (0,1), so the world point is (x, y) + (0, 1).
fn test_quarter_turn() {
    st = stage::stage_new();
    n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_y: 20.0, pl_rot: 1.5707963267948966 });
    st.compose();
    check_pt("+x turns toward +y", st.world_point(n, 1.0, 0.0), 10.0, 21.0);
    check_pt("+y turns toward -x", st.world_point(n, 0.0, 1.0), 9.0, 20.0);
}

// Non-uniform scale must scale the node's own axes, not the world's — so after a
// quarter turn, sx stretches what is now the world's y.
fn test_non_uniform_scale_follows_rotation() {
    st = stage::stage_new();
    n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_rot: 1.5707963267948966, pl_sx: 3.0, pl_sy: 7.0 });
    st.compose();
    check_pt("sx acts along local x", st.world_point(n, 1.0, 0.0), 0.0, 3.0);
    check_pt("sy acts along local y", st.world_point(n, 0.0, 1.0), -7.0, 0.0);
}

// A pivot away from the corner rotates ABOUT the pivot.  Node at (100,100) with
// origin (5,5) and a quarter turn: local (5,5) pins to (100,100), and local (6,5)
// — one step along local +x — lands one step along world +y.
fn test_pivot_rotates_about_the_origin() {
    st = stage::stage_new();
    n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 100.0, pl_y: 100.0, pl_rot: 1.5707963267948966, pl_ox: 5.0, pl_oy: 5.0 });
    st.compose();
    check_pt("pivot pins", st.world_point(n, 5.0, 5.0), 100.0, 100.0);
    check_pt("a step of local +x", st.world_point(n, 6.0, 5.0), 100.0, 101.0);
    check_pt("a step of local +y", st.world_point(n, 5.0, 6.0), 99.0, 100.0);
}

// THE GATE: three deep, with rotation, non-uniform scale and a pivot at every
// level, all moving together.  A sweep that varies one and pins the rest reads as
// proof and is not — this is the cell where a wrong multiply ORDER survives every
// simpler test.
//
// Worked by hand:
//   A: pos (10,0), rot +90, scale (2,1), origin (0,0)
//      A.linear = R90 . S(2,1) = [a=0, b=2, c=-1, d=0];  A.t = (10, 0)
//   B: child of A, pos (3,0) in A's space, rot 0, scale (1,1), origin (0,0)
//      B.local.t = (3,0)  ->  world t = A.linear . (3,0) + A.t = (10, 6)
//      B.linear = A.linear
//   C: child of B, pos (0,2) in B's space, rot 0, scale (1,4), origin (0,1)
//      C.local: A=[1,0,0,4], t = (0,2) - A.(0,1) = (0, -2)
//      C.world.linear = B.linear . C.local.linear = [a=0, b=2, c=-4, d=0]
//      C.world.t = B.linear . (0,-2) + B.t = (0*0 + -1*-2 + 10, 2*0 + 0*-2 + 6)
//                = (12, 6)
//   C's origin (0,1) must therefore land at C.world.linear.(0,1) + C.world.t
//                = (-4 + 12, 0 + 6) = (8, 6)
fn test_three_deep_all_axes_together() {
    st = stage::stage_new();
    q = 1.5707963267948966;
    a = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_rot: q, pl_sx: 2.0 });
    b = st.node_add(a, stage::Place { pl_x: 3.0 });
    c = st.node_add(b, stage::Place { pl_y: 2.0, pl_sy: 4.0, pl_oy: 1.0 });
    st.compose();
    check_pt("A origin", st.world_origin(a), 10.0, 0.0);
    check_pt("B origin", st.world_origin(b), 10.0, 6.0);
    check_pt("C origin", st.world_origin(c), 8.0, 6.0);
    check_pt("C local (1,1)", st.world_point(c, 1.0, 1.0), 8.0, 8.0);
}

// The invariant compose depends on, enforced rather than trusted: a parent must
// already exist.  Without this a child could be composed against an unwritten
// parent and produce a plausible wrong frame instead of an error.
fn test_forward_parent_is_refused() {
    st = stage::stage_new();
    assert(st.node_add(5, stage::Place {}) == -1,
             "a forward parent reference must be refused");
    assert(st.node_add(-2, stage::Place {}) == -1,
             "a parent below -1 must be refused");
    assert(st.node_count() == 0, "a refused add must not append");
}

tests/textfield.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN145 D2 — focus, tab order and the text field, driven by REPLAYED events.
//
// ⚠ Every buffer below was worked out by hand from the keystroke list, and the
// non-ASCII cells are the point: `len(text)` counts CHARACTERS while `s[a..b]`
// slices BYTES, so a caret kept as a byte offset puts that trap inside the edit
// loop.  Every index here is a character count, and the gates say so in text
// where the two differ.
use stage;
use graphics;

const NOMOD = 0;
const SHIFT = 1;      // graphics::MOD_SHIFT
const CTRL  = 2;      // graphics::MOD_CTRL

fn field_node(st: Stage, s: text) -> integer {
  n = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 100.0, pl_h: 12.0 });
  st.compose();
  st.set_field(n, s);
  st.set_focus(n);
  n
}

// THE GATE.  A replayed keystroke sequence produces the exact buffer, and the
// text arriving through `text_event` is what an IME commits.
fn test_replayed_keystrokes_produce_the_exact_buffer() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = field_node(st, "");
  st.text_event("Ada");
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "Ada", "typed: {st.field_text(n)}");
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 3, "caret follows the insertion");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_BACKSPACE, NOMOD);
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "Ad", "backspace: {st.field_text(n)}");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_LEFT, NOMOD);
  st.text_event("m");
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "Amd", "insert in the middle: {st.field_text(n)}");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_END, NOMOD);
  st.text_event("in");
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "Amdin", "at the end: {st.field_text(n)}");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_HOME, NOMOD);
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_DELETE, NOMOD);
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "mdin", "delete at the start: {st.field_text(n)}");
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 0, "and the caret stays put");
}

// ⚠⚠ THE NON-ASCII GATE.  Every one of these is a CHARACTER operation, and each
// would come out differently if the caret were a byte offset.
fn test_editing_counts_characters_and_not_bytes() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = field_node(st, "héllo");
  assert(len("héllo") == 5, "five characters");
  assert(size("héllo") == 6, "…but six bytes — which is the whole trap");

  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 5, "a new field puts the caret after the last CHARACTER");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_HOME, NOMOD);
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_RIGHT, NOMOD);
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_RIGHT, NOMOD);
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 2, "two rights is two characters");
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_BACKSPACE, NOMOD);
  // Removing the 'é' — a two-byte character — must leave exactly "hllo".
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "hllo", "backspace over a 2-byte char: {st.field_text(n)}");
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 1, "and the caret steps back one CHARACTER");

  st.text_event("é");
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "héllo", "and it goes back exactly where it was");

  // A whole IME commit lands as one insertion, several characters wide.
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_END, NOMOD);
  st.text_event("世界");
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "héllo世界", "IME commit: {st.field_text(n)}");
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 7, "five plus two characters, got {st.field_caret(n)}");
  assert(size(st.field_text(n)) == 12, "which is twelve BYTES — the counts differ");
}

// ⚠⚠ THE MODIFIER GATE.  `D0b` measured that `input`'s bindings cannot express a
// modifier; the EVENT carries one, and here is the difference it makes: the same
