// key with and without Shift does two different things.
fn test_the_same_key_with_shift_does_something_else() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = field_node(st, "abcdef");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_LEFT, NOMOD);
  assert(st.field_selection(n) == "", "plain left selects nothing");
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 5, "and just moves");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_LEFT, SHIFT);
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_LEFT, SHIFT);
  assert(st.field_selection(n) == "de", "shift+left selects: {st.field_selection(n)}");
  assert(st.field_mark(n) == 5, "the anchor stayed where the selection began");

  // Typing over a selection replaces it.
  st.text_event("X");
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "abcXf", "typing replaces the selection: {st.field_text(n)}");
}

// Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X / Ctrl+V round-trip through our own clipboard.
fn test_copy_cut_and_paste() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = field_node(st, "hello");
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_A, CTRL);
  assert(st.field_selection(n) == "hello", "ctrl+a selects all");

  st.key_event(KEY_COPY, CTRL);
  assert(st.clipboard() == "hello", "ctrl+c copied: {st.clipboard()}");
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "hello", "and changed nothing");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_END, NOMOD);
  st.key_event(KEY_PASTE, CTRL);
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "hellohello", "ctrl+v pasted: {st.field_text(n)}");

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_A, CTRL);
  st.key_event(KEY_CUT, CTRL);
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "", "ctrl+x emptied it");
  assert(st.clipboard() == "hellohello", "and took the text with it");
}

// Backspace with a selection deletes the SELECTION, not one character — the
// classic off-by-a-lot.
fn test_backspace_over_a_selection_deletes_the_selection() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = field_node(st, "abcdef");
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_HOME, NOMOD);
  for _ in 0..3 { st.key_event(graphics::KEY_RIGHT, SHIFT); }
  assert(st.field_selection(n) == "abc", "three selected");
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_BACKSPACE, NOMOD);
  assert(st.field_text(n) == "def", "the selection went, not one char: {st.field_text(n)}");
  assert(st.field_selection(n) == "", "and nothing is selected now");
}

// ⚠ A plain arrow with a LIVE selection collapses to its near end rather than
// stepping one further.
fn test_an_arrow_collapses_a_selection_to_its_near_end() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = field_node(st, "abcdef");
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_HOME, NOMOD);
  for _ in 0..3 { st.key_event(graphics::KEY_RIGHT, SHIFT); }
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 3 and st.field_mark(n) == 0, "caret 3, anchor 0");
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_LEFT, NOMOD);
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 0, "left collapses to the START of the selection");

  for _ in 0..3 { st.key_event(graphics::KEY_RIGHT, SHIFT); }
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_RIGHT, NOMOD);
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 3, "and right collapses to its END");
}

// Tab order is the DECLARED order, and it wraps in both directions.
fn test_tab_order_follows_the_declared_order() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  b = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  c = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  st.compose();
  st.set_focusable(a, true);
  st.set_focusable(c, true);          // b is deliberately NOT focusable

  assert(st.focused() == -1, "nothing has it to begin with");
  st.focus_next();
  assert(st.focused() == a, "forward from nothing lands on the FIRST");
  st.focus_next();
  assert(st.focused() == c, "and skips the one that cannot take it");
  st.focus_next();
  assert(st.focused() == a, "and wraps");
  st.focus_prev();
  assert(st.focused() == c, "backward wraps the other way");

  st2 = stage_new();
  _ = st2.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  z = st2.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  st2.compose();
  st2.set_focusable(z, true);
  st2.focus_prev();
  assert(st2.focused() == z, "backward from nothing lands on the LAST");
  assert(b == a + 1, "…and b really was between them");
}

// Tab is delivered as a key like any other, and moves the focus rather than
// typing.
fn test_tab_moves_the_focus_and_does_not_type() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  b = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  st.compose();
  st.set_field(a, "one");
  st.set_field(b, "two");
  st.set_focus(a);

  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_TAB, NOMOD);
  assert(st.focused() == b, "tab moved on");
  assert(st.field_text(a) == "one", "and typed nothing into the old field");
  st.key_event(graphics::KEY_TAB, SHIFT);
  assert(st.focused() == a, "shift+tab goes back");

  // Keys reach only the FOCUSED field.
  st.text_event("!");
  assert(st.field_text(a) == "one!", "the focused field took it");
  assert(st.field_text(b) == "two", "the other one did not");
}

// A press moves the keyboard: onto a focusable node, off everything else.
fn test_a_press_moves_the_keyboard() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  b = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_x: 20.0 });
  st.compose();
  st.set_field(a, "");
  st.set_focusable(b, false);

  _ = st.press(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.focused() == a, "pressing the field focused it");
  _ = st.click(5.0, 5.0);
  _ = st.press(25.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.focused() == -1, "pressing a node that cannot take it blurs");
  st.text_event("x");
  assert(st.field_text(a) == "", "and nothing is typed anywhere");
}

// A disabled node cannot hold the keyboard and is skipped by tab.
fn test_a_disabled_node_leaves_the_tab_order() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  b = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  st.compose();
  st.set_focusable(a, true);
  st.set_focusable(b, true);
  st.set_enabled(b, false);

  st.focus_next();
  assert(st.focused() == a, "on the first");
  st.focus_next();
  assert(st.focused() == a, "the disabled one is skipped, so it stays");
  st.set_focus(b);
  assert(st.focused() == -1, "and it cannot be focused directly either");
}

// A click resolves to a CHARACTER index — the seam whoever draws the text uses
// once it has turned an x-coordinate into a count.
fn test_placing_the_caret_by_character_index() {
  st = stage_new();
  n = field_node(st, "héllo");
  st.field_place_caret(n, 2, false);
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 2, "placed at 2");
  assert(st.field_selection(n) == "", "with nothing selected");

  st.field_place_caret(n, 4, true);
  assert(st.field_selection(n) == "ll", "extending selects: {st.field_selection(n)}");

  st.field_place_caret(n, 99, false);
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 5, "a click past the end clamps to the length");
  st.field_place_caret(n, -3, false);
  assert(st.field_caret(n) == 0, "and before the start clamps to zero");
}

tests/views.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN144 P6 — several views over one stage.
//
// The split this enforces is the useful half: the WORLD is deterministic and
// shared, the PRESENTATION is local and free.  Two players may look at one scene
// from two cameras through two viewports, and neither may perturb the other —
// not its pixels, not its picking, and not the single instance upload they share.
use stage;
use graphics;

EPS = 0.000001;
fn near(a: float, b: float) -> boolean { a - b < EPS and b - a < EPS }

fn same(a: const graphics::Canvas, b: const graphics::Canvas) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for i in 0..len(a.data) { if (a.data[i] ?? 0) != (b.data[i] ?? 0) { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}
fn count_of(cv: const graphics::Canvas, want: integer) -> integer {
  n = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v == want { n = n + 1; } }
  n
}

// A mark at world (100, 50), 10 x 10.
fn marked(st: stage::Stage) -> integer {
  st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_x: 100.0, pl_y: 50.0,
                                 pl_colour: 0xc03030 })
}

// ── the degenerate case first ────────────────────────────────────────────────

// A stage nobody split has exactly ONE view, and the old camera calls are that
// view — one code path, as P2's parallax 1.0 is.
fn test_one_view_is_the_default_and_the_old_camera_is_it() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  assert(st.view_count() == 1, "a fresh stage already has its view");
  st.set_camera(7.0, 9.0);
  assert(near(st.view_camera_x(0), 7.0) and near(st.view_camera_y(0), 9.0),
         "set_camera writes view 0");
  st.set_view_camera(0, 3.0, 4.0);
  assert(near(st.camera_x(), 3.0) and near(st.camera_y(), 4.0),
         "and reading it back through the old name agrees");
}

// ── a second view cannot perturb the first ───────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ THE PHASE'S WHOLE CLAIM.  View 0's frame must be byte-identical whether or
// not a second view exists — otherwise split-screen is a different game.
fn test_a_second_view_does_not_change_the_first() {
  alone = stage::stage_new();
  _ = marked(alone);
  alone.set_view_rect(0, 0, 0, 200, 100);
  alone.set_view_camera(0, 60.0, 20.0);
  alone.compose();
  ca = graphics::canvas(400, 100, 0xff000000);
  alone.render_view(0, ca);

  shared = stage::stage_new();
  _ = marked(shared);
  shared.set_view_rect(0, 0, 0, 200, 100);
  shared.set_view_camera(0, 60.0, 20.0);
  v1 = shared.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);
  shared.set_view_camera(v1, -30.0, 5.0);
  shared.compose();
  cb = graphics::canvas(400, 100, 0xff000000);
  shared.render_view(0, cb);

  assert(shared.view_count() == 2, "two views");
  assert(same(ca, cb) == 0,
         "view 0 is byte-identical with a second view present — {same(ca, cb)} differ");
}

// ⚠ TWO VIEWS, ONE UPLOAD.  The scene is packed once and every view draws from
// it; a view that re-packed would spend A3's entire budget on looking twice.
// This is P2's property — the camera is a uniform — carried to N cameras.
fn test_two_views_cost_one_upload() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = marked(st);
  v1 = st.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);
  st.set_view_camera(v1, 40.0, 0.0);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.sync_instances(), "the scene is new, so the first pack is an upload");
  assert(!st.sync_instances(), "nothing changed, so nothing re-packs");
  st.set_view_camera(v1, 90.0, 0.0);
  st.set_view_camera(0, -12.0, 3.0);
  assert(!st.sync_instances(), "moving EITHER camera must not re-pack");
  assert(st.uploads() == 1, "one upload serves both views — got {st.uploads()}");
}

// ── each view sees through its own camera ────────────────────────────────────

// Hand-computed.  The mark sits at world x = 100.  View 0 is the rect
// (0,0,200,100) with its camera at 0, so the mark lands at screen x = 100.
// View 1 is the rect (200,0,200,100) with its camera at 100, so the mark lands
// at 100 - 100 + 200 = 200 — the left edge of that viewport, because the camera
// is looking straight at it.
fn test_each_view_uses_its_own_camera_and_viewport() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = marked(st);
  st.set_view_rect(0, 0, 0, 200, 100);
  v1 = st.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);
  st.set_view_camera(v1, 100.0, 0.0);
  st.compose();

  c0 = graphics::canvas(400, 100, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(0, c0);
  assert(c0.get_pixel(100, 50) == 0xffc03030, "view 0 draws the mark at x=100");
  assert(count_of(c0, 0xffc03030) == 100, "one 10x10 mark, whole");

  c1 = graphics::canvas(400, 100, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(v1, c1);
  assert(c1.get_pixel(200, 50) == 0xffc03030, "view 1 draws it at its left edge");
  assert(c1.get_pixel(100, 50) == 0xff000000,
         "and NOT where view 0 put it — that would be one shared camera");
  assert(count_of(c1, 0xffc03030) == 100, "still whole, just elsewhere");
}

// ⚠ A VIEW PAINTS ONLY INSIDE ITS OWN RECT.  Without that, one player's world
// bleeds across the other's screen, which is the failure everybody sees first.
fn test_a_view_paints_only_inside_its_viewport() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  _ = marked(st);                       // world x 100..110
  v1 = st.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);
  st.set_view_camera(v1, 0.0, 0.0);     // so it wants to draw at 100..110 + 200
  st.set_view_camera(0, 0.0, 0.0);
  st.set_view_rect(0, 0, 0, 200, 100);
  st.compose();

  c1 = graphics::canvas(400, 100, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(v1, c1);
  assert(count_of(c1, 0xffc03030) == 100, "the mark is drawn, inside the rect");
  assert(c1.get_pixel(300, 50) == 0xffc03030, "at 100 + 200, its viewport origin");

  // Now push the camera so the mark would land left of the viewport.
  st.set_view_camera(v1, 150.0, 0.0);   // 100 - 150 + 200 = 150, outside 200..400
  c2 = graphics::canvas(400, 100, 0xff000000);
  st.render_view(v1, c2);
  assert(count_of(c2, 0xffc03030) == 0,
         "outside the rect it paints NOTHING — {count_of(c2, 0xffc03030)} pixels leaked");
}

// ── picking follows the view the point is in ─────────────────────────────────

// ⚠ ONE SCREEN POINT IS A DIFFERENT WORLD POINT IN EACH VIEW — the same reason
// P2 un-applies the camera per LAYER, now per view as well.  A click routed
// through the wrong view picks the wrong entity, silently.
fn test_picking_un_applies_the_view_under_the_point() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  m = marked(st);
  st.set_view_rect(0, 0, 0, 200, 100);
  v1 = st.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);
  st.set_view_camera(v1, 100.0, 0.0);
  st.compose();

  assert(st.pick_in(0, 105.0, 55.0) == m, "view 0: the mark is at screen 100..110");
  assert(st.pick_in(0, 205.0, 55.0) == -1, "and nothing of view 0's is over there");
  assert(st.pick_in(v1, 205.0, 55.0) == m, "view 1: the same mark, at 200..210");
  assert(st.pick_in(v1, 105.0, 55.0) == -1, "not at view 0's screen position");
}

// A point outside a view's rect belongs to no entity OF THAT VIEW, whatever the
// camera would say — the viewport is part of the mapping, not decoration.
//
// ⚠ THE CAMERA IS CHOSEN SO THE GUARD IS THE ONLY THING SAYING NO.  View 1's
// offset is -155 + 200 = 45, so screen x = 150 maps to world x = 105 — squarely
// inside the mark.  Drop the viewport test and this point PICKS IT, through a
// view that does not even paint there.  An earlier version of this test put the
// point where the camera missed anyway, so it passed either way and proved
// nothing; the control is what exposed that.
fn test_a_point_outside_a_view_picks_nothing_in_it() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  m = marked(st);
  st.set_view_rect(0, 0, 0, 200, 100);
  v1 = st.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);
  st.set_view_camera(v1, 155.0, 0.0);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.hits_in(v1, m, 150.0, 55.0),
         "the mark IS under that point as view 1 maps it — 150 - 45 = 105");
  assert(st.pick_in(v1, 150.0, 55.0) == -1,
         "but a point in view 0's half is not view 1's to answer");
}

// The unsplit path is unchanged: `pick` is view 0's pick.
fn test_pick_is_view_zeros_pick() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  m = marked(st);
  st.compose();
  assert(st.pick(105.0, 55.0) == m, "the old entry point still works");
  st.set_camera(50.0, 0.0);
  assert(st.pick(55.0, 55.0) == m, "and still un-applies the camera");
}

// ── presentation is per view, the world is shared ────────────────────────────

// Adding a view adds no nodes, no sequences and no geometry — it is a way of
// LOOKING, and the scene cannot tell how many are looking.
fn test_a_view_is_presentation_not_world() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  m = marked(st);
  st.compose();
  n_before = st.node_count();
  b0 = st.bounds_of(m);
  _ = st.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);
  _ = st.add_view(0, 100, 400, 100);
  st.compose();
  b1 = st.bounds_of(m);
  assert(st.node_count() == n_before, "three views, no extra nodes");
  assert(near(b0.b_x0, b1.b_x0) and near(b0.b_y0, b1.b_y0), "and the world did not move");
  assert(near(b0.b_x1, b1.b_x1) and near(b0.b_y1, b1.b_y1), "in either corner");
}

// ── the GL path's own arithmetic ─────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// ⚠ GATED DIRECTLY, because `gl_render` needs a GL context these tests do not
// have.  The scissor rect is the one piece of view arithmetic the software path
// does NOT share — it narrows integer spans where GL cuts with a rect — so a
// wrong answer here is a silent wrong picture on the target that ships.  Every
// rect below is worked out by hand.
fn batch_at(clipped: boolean, x0: float, y0: float, x1: float, y1: float) -> stage::Batch {
  stage::Batch { b_layer: 0, b_atlas: 0, b_first: 0, b_count: 1,
                 b_clipped: clipped, b_cx0: x0, b_cy0: y0, b_cx1: x1, b_cy1: y1 }
}
fn zero() -> stage::P2 { stage::P2 { p_x: 0.0, p_y: 0.0 } }

fn test_the_scissor_is_the_view_rect_met_with_the_clip() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  v1 = st.add_view(200, 0, 200, 100);

  // An unbounded view and an unclipped run: nothing is cut.
  a = st.scissor_of(0, batch_at(false, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0), zero());
  assert(near(a.b_x0, 0.0) and a.b_x1 > 100000.0, "view 0 unbounded, run unclipped");

  // A bounded view, unclipped run: exactly the view's rect.
  b = st.scissor_of(v1, batch_at(false, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0), zero());
  assert(near(b.b_x0, 200.0) and near(b.b_x1, 400.0), "the viewport, in x");
  assert(near(b.b_y0, 0.0) and near(b.b_y1, 100.0), "and in y");

  // Unbounded view, clipped run: the clip alone.
  c = st.scissor_of(0, batch_at(true, 10.0, 10.0, 50.0, 60.0), zero());
  assert(near(c.b_x0, 10.0) and near(c.b_x1, 50.0), "the clip, in x");
  assert(near(c.b_y0, 10.0) and near(c.b_y1, 60.0), "and in y");

  // Both: the INTERSECTION, not the innermost — the rule A6 already fixed.
  d = st.scissor_of(v1, batch_at(true, 100.0, 10.0, 300.0, 60.0), zero());
  assert(near(d.b_x0, 200.0) and near(d.b_x1, 300.0),
         "met with the viewport: {d.b_x0}..{d.b_x1}, want 200..300");

  // ⚠ And the clip MOVES WITH ITS CONTENT.  A camera 20 to the right shifts the
  // cut 20 to the left, exactly as the software path shifts it.
  e = st.scissor_of(0, batch_at(true, 100.0, 0.0, 200.0, 50.0),
                    stage::P2 { p_x: -20.0, p_y: 0.0 });
  assert(near(e.b_x0, 80.0) and near(e.b_x1, 180.0),
         "panned clip: {e.b_x0}..{e.b_x1}, want 80..180");

  // Disjoint reads as EMPTY, so the caller can tell "cut everything" from "cut
  // nothing" — an inverted rect handed to GL is undefined behaviour.
  f = st.scissor_of(v1, batch_at(true, 0.0, 0.0, 50.0, 50.0), zero());
  assert(f.b_x1 <= f.b_x0, "a run outside the view leaves an empty rect");
}

tests/widgetstate.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// @PLN145 D1 — the four widget states, driven by a REPLAYED pointer stream.
//
// The stream is data, so the gate is a recording rather than a script of calls:
// every expected state below was worked out by hand from the event list, and a
// failure prints the whole sequence rather than one cell.
//
// ⚠ The pointer path needed no new seam — `press` / `release` / `pointer_move`
// take coordinates and read no device, which is the constraint @PLN144 `A4` was
// built under.  (The KEY half is the `input` package's `input_tick_from_state`,
// cleared by `D0b`.)
use stage;

const MOVE = 0;
const DOWN = 1;
const UP   = 2;

struct Ev { e_kind: integer, e_x: float, e_y: float }

fn ev(k: integer, x: float, y: float) -> Ev { Ev { e_kind: k, e_x: x, e_y: y } }

fn name_of(s: UiState) -> text {
  match s {
    Normal   => "Normal",
    Over     => "Over",
    Down     => "Down",
    Disabled => "Disabled",
  }
}

// Two buttons, side by side: A over x 0..10, B over x 20..30.
fn two_buttons(st: Stage) -> integer {
  a = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  _ = st.node_add(-1, Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0, pl_x: 20.0 });
  st.compose();
  a
}

// Apply one event; answer the node it fired, or -1.
fn apply(st: Stage, e: const Ev) -> integer {
  if e.e_kind == MOVE { st.pointer_move(e.e_x, e.e_y); return -1; }
  if e.e_kind == DOWN { _ = st.press(e.e_x, e.e_y); return -1; }
  st.click(e.e_x, e.e_y)
}

fn the_stream() -> vector<Ev> {
  [ev(MOVE, 5.0, 5.0),      // 1  onto A
   ev(DOWN, 5.0, 5.0),      // 2  press A
   ev(MOVE, 25.0, 5.0),     // 3  drag off A, across B
   ev(UP,   25.0, 5.0),     // 4  release away — must NOT fire
   ev(MOVE, 5.0, 5.0),      // 5  back onto A
   ev(DOWN, 5.0, 5.0),      // 6  press A again
   ev(MOVE, 6.0, 6.0),      // 7  drift one pixel, still inside
   ev(UP,   6.0, 6.0)]      // 8  release inside — MUST fire
}

// THE GATE.  Eight events, and the exact state of both buttons after each.
fn test_a_replayed_stream_drives_the_exact_state_sequence() {
  // Hand-computed from the stream above.  B is `Normal` at step 3 because a
  // press in flight suppresses `Over` everywhere — dragging across a neighbour
  // must not light it up.
  want_a = ["Over", "Down", "Normal", "Normal", "Over", "Down", "Down", "Over"];
  want_b = ["Normal", "Normal", "Normal", "Over", "Normal", "Normal", "Normal", "Normal"];

  st = stage_new();
  a = two_buttons(st);
  b = a + 1;
  fired: vector<integer> = [];
  for e in the_stream() {
    fired += [apply(st, e)];
    k = e#index;
    got_a = name_of(st.ui_state(a));
    got_b = name_of(st.ui_state(b));
    assert(got_a == (want_a[k] ?? ""), "step {k + 1}: A is {got_a}, expected {want_a[k] ?? \"\"}");
    assert(got_b == (want_b[k] ?? ""), "step {k + 1}: B is {got_b}, expected {want_b[k] ?? \"\"}");
  }
  assert((fired[3] ?? 0) == -1, "step 4 released away from A and must NOT fire");
  assert((fired[7] ?? 0) == a, "step 8 released inside A and MUST fire");
}

// ⚠⚠ THE INVARIANT: a node reads `Down` exactly when a release at the current
// pointer would fire it.  Swept over five release positions — the picture and
// the click come from one predicate, so they cannot disagree.
fn test_down_means_exactly_that_a_release_would_fire() {
  xs = [5.0, 9.5, 11.0, 25.0, 100.0];
  ys = [5.0, 9.5, 5.0,  5.0,  100.0];
  downs = 0;
  fires = 0;
  for i in 0..len(xs) {
    st = stage_new();
    a = two_buttons(st);
    _ = st.press(5.0, 5.0);                       // always press A's middle
    px = xs[i] ?? 0.0;
    py = ys[i] ?? 0.0;
    st.pointer_move(px, py);
    looks_down = st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Down;
    did_fire = st.click(px, py) == a;
    assert(looks_down == did_fire,
           "at ({px}, {py}) the button looked {name_of(st.ui_state(a))} but firing was {did_fire}");
    if looks_down { downs = downs + 1; }
    if did_fire { fires = fires + 1; }
  }
  // ⚠ The control: the sweep must contain BOTH answers, or the equivalence above
  // is satisfied by everything being false.
  assert(downs == 2, "two of the five positions are inside A, got {downs}");
  assert(fires == 2, "and exactly those two fire");
}

// ⚠⚠ ON TOUCH THERE IS NO `Over` — replayed on the SAME stream, so what changes
// is only what is SHOWN, never what fires.
fn test_touch_never_shows_over_and_fires_the_same() {
  mouse_fired: vector<integer> = [];
  st_m = stage_new();
  a_m = two_buttons(st_m);
  for e in the_stream() { mouse_fired += [apply(st_m, e)]; }
  assert(a_m == 0, "both runs build the same scene, so the node ids line up");

  touch_fired: vector<integer> = [];
  st_t = stage_new();
  a_t = two_buttons(st_t);
  st_t.set_pointer(POINTER_TOUCH);
  overs = 0;
  for e in the_stream() {
    touch_fired += [apply(st_t, e)];
    if st_t.ui_state(a_t) == UiState.Over { overs = overs + 1; }
    if st_t.ui_state(a_t + 1) == UiState.Over { overs = overs + 1; }
  }

  assert(overs == 0, "a touch stream showed `Over` {overs} times");
  assert(len(mouse_fired) == len(touch_fired), "same stream, same length");
  for i in 0..len(mouse_fired) {
    assert((mouse_fired[i] ?? 0) == (touch_fired[i] ?? 0),
           "step {i + 1} fired differently on touch");
  }
  assert((touch_fired[7] ?? 0) == a_t, "and the touch DID fire on the last step");

  // ⚠ The control: the same stream on a MOUSE does show `Over`, so the zero
  // above is the pointer kind and not a stream that never hovers.
  mouse_overs = 0;
  st_c = stage_new();
  a_c = two_buttons(st_c);
  for e in the_stream() {
    _ = apply(st_c, e);
    if st_c.ui_state(a_c) == UiState.Over { mouse_overs = mouse_overs + 1; }
  }
  // Steps 1, 5 and 8 — onto A, back onto A, and after the release clears the
  // capture with the pointer still on it.
  assert(mouse_overs == 3, "the mouse run shows Over three times, got {mouse_overs}");
}

// Leaving and coming back RE-ARMS: the capture is held the whole time, so the
// press is not lost by drifting off and back.
fn test_leaving_and_returning_re_arms() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = two_buttons(st);
  _ = st.press(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Down, "pressed");
  st.pointer_move(50.0, 50.0);
  assert(st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Normal, "away, so not armed");
  assert(st.capture() == a, "…but the press is still held");
  st.pointer_move(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Down, "back inside, armed again");
  assert(st.click(5.0, 5.0) == a, "and it fires");
}

// `Disabled` outranks the other three, and refuses to fire.
fn test_a_disabled_node_never_fires_and_always_looks_disabled() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = two_buttons(st);
  st.set_enabled(a, false);
  assert(!st.enabled(a), "it is off");

  st.pointer_move(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Disabled, "hovering it does not make it Over");
  _ = st.press(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Disabled, "pressing it does not make it Down");
  assert(st.click(5.0, 5.0) == -1, "and it does not fire");

  st.set_enabled(a, true);
  st.pointer_move(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Over, "and it comes back");
}

// `release` is untouched by D1 — it still answers who took the press, whether or
// not the pointer is still there.  `click` is the other door.
fn test_release_still_answers_the_captured_node() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = two_buttons(st);
  _ = st.press(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.release(50.0, 50.0) == a, "release names the node that took the press");

  _ = st.press(5.0, 5.0);
  assert(st.click(50.0, 50.0) == -1, "while click names the node that FIRES");
}

// A press on nothing captures nothing, and a release then fires nothing.
fn test_pressing_empty_space_fires_nothing() {
  st = stage_new();
  a = two_buttons(st);
  _ = st.press(50.0, 50.0);
  assert(st.capture() == -1, "nothing was under the pointer");
  assert(st.ui_state(a) == UiState.Normal, "and no button is lit");
  assert(st.click(5.0, 5.0) == -1, "releasing over a button does not fire it");
}

tests/worked-examples.loft

// Copyright (c) 2026 Jurjen Stellingwerff
// SPDX-License-Identifier: LGPL-3.0-or-later
//
// Worked examples for the `stage` library (@PLN141).  Each `test_*` below
// demonstrates a contract the signature cannot state; the function documents
// itself by CITING the tag with `// Example: @STG-00N`, and the doc-drift gate
// fails if a citation goes dangling.  Distinct from the assertion suite in
// `stage.loft`.
//
// Everything here is floats in and floats out, so the type checker sees none of
// it.  What a caller gets wrong is WHICH POINT a node is anchored by, that the
// tree is an ordered array rather than a graph, and that reading a world
// transform before composing answers a stale one rather than an error.
use stage;
use graphics;

EPS = 0.0001;
fn close(a: float, b: float) -> boolean { a - b < EPS and b - a < EPS }

// @STG-001 — a node is anchored by its ORIGIN, not by its top-left corner.
// `pl_ox`/`pl_oy` name a point in the node's own units, and `compose` places
// exactly that point at (`pl_x`, `pl_y`) — whatever the rotation and scale are.
// Put the origin at a sprite's feet and the sprite STANDS UP from where it was
// placed, which is what lets a 2-D scene present a 3-D world: the footprint
// decides where it sits and the artwork decides nothing.  Move the origin and the
// node pivots about the new point rather than sliding, so the same `pl_x`/`pl_y`
// mean a different picture — the commonest surprise in this library.
fn test_origin_is_what_a_node_is_anchored_by() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  feet = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 50.0, pl_y: 80.0, pl_ox: 8.0, pl_oy: 16.0,
                                        pl_rot: 0.7, pl_sx: 2.0, pl_sy: 3.0 });
  st.compose();
  at = st.world_origin(feet);
  assert(close(at.p_x, 50.0) and close(at.p_y, 80.0),
         "the origin lands on (pl_x, pl_y) under any rotation and scale");
}

// @STG-002 — the tree is a FLAT ARRAY and a parent must already exist.  A child
// names its parent by index, and `node_add` REFUSES a forward reference by
// answering -1 rather than accepting it.  That is not bookkeeping: `compose` is a
// single forward pass, so a child sitting before its parent would read a world
// transform that had not been written yet and produce a plausible wrong frame —
// the kind of bug that looks like an art problem.  Build parents first and the
// invariant holds by construction.
fn test_a_parent_must_already_exist() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  assert(st.node_add(3, stage::Place {}) == -1, "a forward parent reference is refused");
  root = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 1.0 });
  child = st.node_add(root, stage::Place { pl_x: 2.0 });
  assert(root == 0 and child == 1, "indices are assigned in insertion order");
  assert(st.node_count() == 2, "a refused add appends nothing");
}

// @STG-003 — a world transform is DERIVED, and reading one before `compose` gives
// the identity rather than an error.  Mutating a node is cheap precisely because
// nothing recomputes on write; the cost is that `compose` is the caller's to run,
// once per frame, after the last mutation.  Skip it and the frame renders the
// PREVIOUS state — every node in the right relative place, the whole scene one
// step behind, which reads as input lag rather than as a missing call.
fn test_compose_is_the_callers_to_run() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  n = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_y: 20.0 });
  before = st.world_origin(n);
  assert(close(before.p_x, 0.0) and close(before.p_y, 0.0),
         "before compose, the world transform is still the identity");
  st.compose();
  after = st.world_origin(n);
  assert(close(after.p_x, 10.0) and close(after.p_y, 20.0), "compose derives it");
}

// @STG-004 — the batcher merges ADJACENT runs and never sorts, and the cost of
// that is real.  2-D correctness is painter's algebra: overlapping translucent
// draws must composite in call order, so gathering every command of one atlas
// together would be faster and would draw the wrong picture.  The consequence a
// caller must plan for is that interleaving two atlases sprite-by-sprite gives
// ONE INSTANCE PER RUN — four sprites, four draws.  That is a PACKING problem and
// the cure is upstream: sprites that draw near each other must share a page.
// Batching cannot fix it without reordering, and reordering is the one thing it
// may not do.
fn test_the_batcher_never_reorders() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  a = stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 1 };
  b = stage::Place { pl_w: 4.0, pl_h: 4.0, pl_atlas: 2 };
  _ = st.node_add(-1, a);
  _ = st.node_add(-1, b);
  _ = st.node_add(-1, a);
  _ = st.node_add(-1, b);
  st.compose();
  assert(len(st.batches()) == 4,
         "interleaved atlases stay four runs — gathering them would reorder the frame");

  grouped = stage::stage_new();
  _ = grouped.node_add(-1, a);
  _ = grouped.node_add(-1, a);
  _ = grouped.node_add(-1, b);
  _ = grouped.node_add(-1, b);
  grouped.compose();
  assert(len(grouped.batches()) == 2, "the same four sprites, packed well, are two runs");
}

// @STG-005 — a release belongs to the node that took the PRESS, not to whatever
// is under the pointer now.  This is the half of input routing that a hit test
// alone does not give you, and leaving it out produces two bugs that both read as
// "the button is broken": it fires when you press it and slide away, and it fails
// to fire when you press it and drift a single pixel.  `release` therefore answers
// the CAPTURED node wherever the pointer went, and `released_on` is the separate
// question a button actually asks — did the release land back on me.
fn test_a_release_belongs_to_the_press() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  btn = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 10.0, pl_h: 10.0 });
  st.compose();
  assert(st.press(5.0, 5.0) == btn, "the press lands");
  assert(st.release(900.0, 900.0) == btn, "the release still belongs to it");
  assert(!st.released_on(btn, 900.0, 900.0), "but it did not release ON it — no fire");
  assert(st.press(5.0, 5.0) == btn and st.released_on(btn, 6.0, 6.0), "this one fires");
}

// @STG-006 — a sprite is mostly holes, so picking samples its ALPHA and a click
// over a transparent texel reaches what is behind.  A node with no mask is solid,
// which is what a panel or a tile wants; give a node a mask and its shape becomes
// its art rather than its rectangle.  You can see through the tree, so you can
// click through it.
fn test_a_click_falls_through_a_hole() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  behind = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 40.0, pl_h: 40.0 });
  holed = st.add_mask(2, 2, [255, 255, 255, 0]);
  front = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 40.0, pl_h: 40.0, pl_mask: holed });
  st.compose();
  assert(st.pick(5.0, 5.0) == front, "the opaque quadrant is the front node");
  assert(st.pick(35.0, 35.0) == behind, "the hole reaches what is behind");
}

// @STG-007 — a clip INHERITS and INTERSECTS, and a clip region is sized but
// invisible.  Nesting two clippers gives the overlap of the two, never the inner
// one alone: a panel inside a scrolling list is bounded by both, which is what
// makes a scroll view composable.  The idiom for the region itself is `pl_clips`
// with a size and `pl_visible: false` — it needs a rectangle to define the bound
// and no paint of its own.  ⚠ The bound is AXIS-ALIGNED: a rotated clipper clips
// to its bounding box, because a hardware scissor is axis-aligned and there is no
// honest way to pretend otherwise.
fn test_a_clip_inherits_and_intersects() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  outer = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_w: 20.0, pl_h: 20.0, pl_clips: true,
                                         pl_visible: false });
  inner = st.node_add(outer, stage::Place { pl_x: 10.0, pl_y: 10.0, pl_w: 30.0,
                                            pl_h: 30.0, pl_clips: true,
                                            pl_visible: false });
  _ = st.node_add(inner, stage::Place { pl_w: 60.0, pl_h: 60.0, pl_colour: 0x00ff00 });
  st.compose();
  cv = graphics::canvas(48, 48, 0xff000000);
  st.render_stage(cv);
  n = 0;
  for v in cv.data { if v == 0xff00ff00 { n = n + 1; } }
  assert(n == 100, "the overlap of (0,0)-(20,20) and (10,10)-(40,40) is 10x10");
}

// @STG-008 — ambient motion costs NOTHING per frame, and that is a design
// constraint rather than an optimisation.  A tuft's phase is derived from where it
// stands and its motion from one clock, so a field of five hundred is a buffer
// uploaded once plus a uniform that ticks; storing and stepping a phase would
// rewrite all five hundred every frame.  Two consequences a caller should expect:
// neighbours are out of step (a field looks like a field rather than like the
// ground moving), and sway is VISUAL ONLY — a swaying tree keeps its depth, its
// bounds and its hit area, because one that re-sorted as it swayed would flicker
// past its neighbours and grass that dodged the pointer would be worse than grass
// that ignored it.
fn test_ambient_motion_is_free_and_visual_only() {
  st = stage::stage_new();
  a = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 0.0, pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 6.0, pl_sway: 4.0 });
  b = st.node_add(-1, stage::Place { pl_x: 9.0, pl_w: 2.0, pl_h: 6.0, pl_sway: 4.0 });
  st.compose();
  before = st.pack_instances();
  st.set_time(1.4);
  after = st.pack_instances();
  for i in 0..len(before) { assert(before[i] == after[i], "the clock rewrites nothing"); }
  assert(st.sway_of(a) != st.sway_of(b), "and neighbours are out of step");
  bounds = st.bounds_of(a);
  st.set_time(2.9);
  moved = st.bounds_of(a);
  assert(bounds.b_x0 == moved.b_x0, "the footprint never moves with the sway");
}
